### Deploys, Servers & Deployment Gotchas

#### SERP AUTO-DEPLOYS from `main` — never deploy by hand

⚠️ **Flipped from the pre-cutover myth.** AI (working from old notes) assumes SERP deploys manually via `ssh … 'bash deploy-k8s.sh main'`. **Wrong now:** every push/merge to SERP `main` **auto-deploys** — GitHub webhook → Jenkins (`ciservice.sugarwish.com`, AWS `18.205.218.252`) SSHes to the Hetzner K3s node `5.161.95.56` (authenticates as root, runs the script as `jack` — it refuses uid 0) and runs `bash deploy-k8s.sh main` (steps [1/6]..[6/6], includes `migrate:serp-app` against `serp_app`) ~1s after the push → ArgoCD rolls the new tags. **A merge to `main` ships; don't start a second deploy by hand.** Branch flow: feature → `dev` → `staging` → `main` (PRs target `dev`; `hotfix/*` may go to `main` and **must back-merge to `dev`** in the same session).

⚠️ **Machines, not one:** production SERP runs on the **Hetzner K3s cluster** (node `5.161.95.56`, namespace `serp`, `/opt/SERP`); production moved to a single dedicated server with the order queue as an independent process (9/29 touchbase). The old **AWS EC2 `34.203.231.65`** (`/opt/SERP`, PM2 + nginx, `deploy.sh`) is **frozen legacy — NEVER deploy to it.** The MySQL host is a **separate Hetzner machine `5.161.233.240`** holding `serp_app` (prod) and `serp_test` (staging).

⚠️ **Schema changes ship as committed migrations applied by the runner** (no manual DBA step, no "migrate the live manage DB before code ships"). `_migrations` versions are timestamp-named `YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS_desc`; `serp_app` and `serp_test` heads are **independent** (last observed heads: see Databases) — a gap is the normal staging-first steady state, not a failed migration. Check live before claiming "same head" or "behind"; there is no numeric "078" version anymore.

⚠️ **Never hand-run `npm run migrate:serp-app` to "verify locally".** The runner (`serp_app/run-serp-app-migrations.js`) targets whatever `SERP_APP_DB_*` in `serp_app/.env` says and **ignores `SERP_ORM_ENV`** — it has applied a migration to live `serp_test` that way, and a worktree whose `.env` was copied from the main clone printed `→ serp_app` (production). The banner line shows the real target. Local path: `node serp_app/run-serp-app-migrations.js --local --db=serp_local_prod` (`--status` first). Only the deploy applies migrations to the deployed DBs.

⚠️ **Post-cutover, fix `serp_app` data with a migration, never a seeder change.** `serp_app` is no longer reseeded from Odoo — `seed.js` / `db:full-reseed` now refuse both deployed DBs (`serp_app`, `serp_test`; commit `a42b889e4`), and `/reseed-serp-test` is retired — so a seeder fix changes nothing live. Seeded-data gaps (e.g. return moves' `to_refund`, backfilled 2026-09-27) are repaired by a committed `serp_app` migration.

⚠️ **`serp_*` table location:** `serp_app` and `serp_test` hold the full ORM table set. `laravel_live` holds only a thin bridge (`serp_published_kits`, `serp_published_stock`, `serp_mrp_bom` kits, plus leftover `serp_*` tables slated for removal — target state, owner Manish, **NOT done**: drop `serp_*` tables from `laravel_live` except the two published tables, remove inventory transactions + deprecated columns + related Laravel code, remove Odoo inbound/outbound sidecars and SERP's Odoo connection code). `laravel_local` (local dev) carries its own copy; local Docker DBs are `serp_local_staging` / `serp_local_prod`.

#### SWAC / WishDesk MCP and staging deploy gotchas

⚠️ **SWAC MCP server stale code bug:** Jenkins deploy did **not** restart the MCP server (~Oct–Nov 2025), so it served stale code/tool counts (Jason saw 28 local tools, only 23 on desk2). Standing rule: "when we make MCP changes we also request Seth restart the MCP server." Now fixed by moving to PM2. There is **one** MCP server (~port 3001 at `/mcp`), **not** per-env.

⚠️ **SWAC `desk3` (staging) points at LIVE databases** — data is NOT isolated. Branch flow is `feature → development → staging → live` (**NOT `main`**). `desk2`=dev, `desk3`=staging (live data), `desk`=live.

#### Connection / pool / event-loop footguns

⚠️ **Retool PG is one pool module (`pools/retool.py`) with two halves** — asyncpg (min 2 / max 10) + psycopg2 (min 2 / max 10); the old "three pools, 15+5+5" layout is gone. The SERP ORM pool connection is held idle during entire 200–320s forecast requests — only **13 concurrent requests exhaust the ORM pool** (`database.py`: `pool_size=5` + `max_overflow=8` = 13 max; the routed pool in `pools/mysql_routed.py` is also 5+8). `pool_recycle=300s` is deliberate so a 200–320s forecast query isn't recycled mid-flight. (Odoo had TWO pools pre-cutover; SERP no longer reads Odoo.)

⚠️ **Sync items stuck in `processing` after a restart are recovered by a sweeper, not lost.** Old notes said a gunicorn restart left `processing` items **never recovered** (no heartbeat/TTL/startup sweeper) — fixed 2026-03-06 (commit `63b0d9743`): `workers/serpy_sync_worker.py` calls `_recover_stuck(startup=True)` on start and again every `STALE_RECOVERY_INTERVAL_POLLS` (20) polls; recovered rows get ` [Recovered from stuck processing]` appended to `error_message` (`services/serpy_sync_queue/service.py`). A handler that dies mid-op can still leave partial side effects, so check that tag before replaying. **Timed-out items are NOT re-run** (PR #1565, 2026-09-28): a worker cycle is capped at `CYCLE_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 180`; a cycle that exceeds it stamps its in-flight item `[cycle timeout]` (`CYCLE_TIMEOUT_MARKER`), and recovery moves stamped items to **`failed` for manual verification** instead of re-queuing a write that may still commit. A handler that committed records a durable `error_details.handler_committed` flag in its own transaction — if it is set, the op already landed; never replay it.

⚠️ **SERP backend resource exhaustion (historical, on the now-frozen AWS EC2 `i-0a0de24d0845c6341`):** under load the SERP backend returned 504s; forecast 500s correlated with ~3.7GB RAM exhaustion + `sa_projections` timeout + missing indexes. _(Symptoms recorded pre-Hetzner; production now runs on the Hetzner K3s cluster, so the `t3.medium+` resize is no longer the remediation. The query/index footguns still apply.)_

#### Repos: each has its own branch/promotion/ticket convention — `main` is rarely safe

⚠️ **AI assumes** uniform git-flow with `main` as the safe default.
✅ **Reality:** SERP: feature → `dev` → `staging` → `main`, and **a push to `main` auto-deploys** (hotfix/* may target `main`; back-merge to `dev`). SWAC/WishDesk: `feature → development → staging → live` (tickets WD-/WW-). sugarwish-laravel: `SUG-*`/`WW-*` feature branches → `manage` (staging) → `blue` (prod). sugarwish-odoo: `staging_new → main` (Odoo is frozen; this repo is history). SWAC/Laravel deliberately **bundle unrelated features per PR**. Verify the table before assuming a branch.

#### WW-106 B-SKU filter regression: a failed blue deploy left split behavior

⚠️ A B-SKU filter added to `updateInsertKit()` (Apr 7-8) dropped receiver-order B- rows to zero, but preselect-order B- rows kept leaking 2–73/day because the **deploy-to-blue Jenkins jobs 3231-3232 FAILED** — blue ran pre-filter code while green/main had the filter, and load-balancing split traffic. Lesson: a "deployed" fix isn't live until ALL servers actually updated.

---

### Order Pipelines & Sync Flags

#### Order-ingest workers (post-cutover)

⚠️ **AI assumes** one unified order queue, that all workers behave identically, or that all SERP sale orders have a populated `odoo_id`.
✅ **Reality:**

- **SERP order ingest — `serp_order_queue_worker.py` is LIVE** (not retired; only the "darklaunch / dual-write / `serp_prod_darklaunch`" framing is gone). It ingests Laravel `ec_order` → `serp_sale_order` (~1,905 orders since 9/27, all `odoo_id` NULL), writing `serp_app`/`serp_test` ONLY, and owns rebuilding `serp_published_kits`/`serp_published_stock`. No queue table — it polls MySQL directly, per-order handlers strictly serial, keyed on the `ec_order.serp_sync` flag (new = `serp_sync=0`; shipped = `serp_sync=1 AND ship_date_serp_synchronized=0`). Pacing knobs (`workers/order_processing.py` / the worker): `ORDER_QUEUE_POLL_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default 300 — only the **idle/error** sleep; busy cycles chain immediately, so it is NOT a throughput cap), `ORDER_QUEUE_DETECTION_LIMIT` (5000), `ORDER_QUEUE_SHIPMENT_LIMIT` (50 per cycle), `ORDER_QUEUE_BUSY_PAUSE_SECONDS` (0). There is no `BATCH_LIMIT`. Run/pause lever is `ORDER_QUEUE_WORKER_ENABLED`. Order-processing code lives in `workers/order_processing.py`.
- **Serpy sync queue** (`workers/serpy_sync_worker.py`, package `services/serpy_sync_queue/`, table `serpy_sync_queue` in `serp_app`): triggered when a Serpy draft is approved; since the cutover **all ops route to `serp:<entity>` handlers**. Live `sync_target` values since 9/26: `serp`, `multi`, `laravel` — the last `odoo` target was created 2026-09-25 (live check: newest `odoo` row 2026-09-25 22:06). **PO receipts flow HERE**, not through the order worker. The old `odoo_sync_worker.py` / `services/odoo_sync_queue/` are gone, and SERP commit `90e712f39` (2026-09-26) **deleted the Odoo XML-RPC handler arms** and `/queue-test`. What remains on `origin/main`: `services/odoo_xmlrpc_service.py` (read-only), the `ODOO_*` config, `workers/odoo_parity_reports/`, `serpy/ops/schemas/odoo/` (Jack is removing the rest of SERP's Odoo connection logic; Laravel `origin/main` likewise still carries dead Odoo code — `app/Sidecar/SynchronizeOdooInventory.php`, `OdooInventoryService.php`, `Http/Controllers/Odoo/**` — removal planned, Manish). The `ODOO_SYNC_WORKER_ENABLED` config name still exists.
- **Merchandise Order Queue (removed):** the separate SERP-native `order_queue_worker.py` (polled `component_orders WHERE order_type='merchandise' AND inventory_source='serp'`) **no longer exists** on `origin/main`; its `ORDER_QUEUE_WORKER_ENABLED` flag (default False in `config.py`) was repurposed as the run/pause lever for `serp_order_queue_worker`. That worker excludes standalone `merchandise` orders (it detects `receiver-order`/`preselect-order` lines) and reads no `inventory_source` — `components.inventory_source` and `component_orders.inventory_source` are `(deleted)`-renamed at cutover (WW-2994), so an `inventory_source=…` predicate throws "Unknown column" on live.
- **Asymmetry:** SERP-native orders intentionally get `NULL` `odoo_id`s — post-cutover that is the norm, not a defect.
- ⚠️ **`serp_sync=1` does NOT prove SERP has the order.** The new-order detector only picks up `serp_sync=0`, so a Laravel flag pointing at a `serp_sale_order` that no longer exists is never re-ingested. The 9/26 cutover reseed rebuilt `serp_app` from frozen Odoo and dropped every shadow-only order: ~39 `ec_order`s created 9/14–9/25 (Odoo never imported them) and 28 more from 9/24–9/25 sat at `serp_sync=1` with no SERP sale order; when such an order ships, `process_shipment` logs "shipped (0 pickings validated)" and still sets `ship_date_serp_synchronized=1` — no reservation, no stock deduction, no COGS. Jack declined a blanket `serp_sync` reset (some were later remediated individually). The lesson: after any `serp_app` reseed or restore, reconcile Laravel `serp_sync=1` orders against `serp_sale_order.ec_order_id` / `preselect_order_id`; re-ingest is `serp_sync=0` (+ `ship_date_serp_synchronized=0` if shipped), and it's Jack's call.

#### The `ec_order` sync flag: `oddo_synchronized` (typo) → replaced by `serp_sync`

⚠️ **Live `ec_order` (laravel_live) no longer has a usable `oddo_synchronized`** (the misspelling is now history — see below) — the column is annotated `oddo_synchronized(deleted)`, as are `component_imported(deleted)` and `ship_date_odoo_synchronized(deleted)`; the live flags are **`serp_sync`** and **`ship_date_serp_synchronized`**. Live `serp_sync` distribution over the newest 100K `ec_order` ids: `1`=synced ~91.8K, `0`=not synced ~3.0K, `4`=cancelled-and-synced ~4.0K, `5`=vendor/special ~1.1K, `2`=in-flight/partial 29. `4` arrived with WW-3013's historical backfill (`CASE WHEN oddo_synchronized=5 THEN 5 WHEN oddo_synchronized=2 THEN 2 WHEN oddo_synchronized=1 AND status='canceled' THEN 4 WHEN oddo_synchronized=1 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END`) — treating the flag as only `0/1/5` miscounts cancelled orders as still-pending. Since 9/26 the live distribution is `1`=1,919, `0`=68, `4`=90, `5`=8. The other Odoo flags — `component_imported`, `ship_date_odoo_synchronized`, the `preselect_orders` odoo flags and `items.odoo_sync` — are likewise `(deleted)`-renamed on `laravel_live`; the old names now throw "Unknown column". Item-level sync reads its PARENT order's `serp_sync`. `manage` (Laravel staging) now matches `laravel_live`: `oddo_synchronized(deleted)`, `ship_date_odoo_synchronized(deleted)`, `serp_sync`, `ship_date_serp_synchronized` — still describe the table in the environment you are querying.

⚠️ **Raw SQL outside Laravel breaks silently on these column changes.** `ec_order.is_printed` is **gone from live `ec_order` entirely** (not even `(deleted)`-renamed; Laravel's `database/skeema/ec_order.sql` still lists it) — print state lives in **`receiver_production_slips.is_printed`** (keyed by `order_id`; production-slip fields moved there Jan 2026), plus `preselect_orders.is_printed` and `print_groups.is_printed`. The n8n **"Duplicate Receiver Orders"** workflow (`EfCt429Vle67ckY8`) still selects `eo.is_printed` and fails every 10 min with `Unknown column 'eo.is_printed'` (ER_BAD_FIELD_ERROR) — so no duplicate-order alerts fire — and its canned cancel query still sets `oddo_synchronized`/`component_imported`, which would also throw. n8n/Retool SQL is not covered by Laravel's tests: after any `laravel_live` column rename/drop, check n8n error executions (`mcp__n8n__list_executions status=error`) before trusting an alert channel's silence.

⚠️ **Reading old data / old code:** the column is spelled **`oddo_synchronized`** (double-d, missing the second o) — **NOT** `odoo_synchronized`; match exactly. Historical values: `0`=not synced (~77K), `1`=synced (~3.88M), `2`=in-flight/partial (~64–166), `5`=vendor/special (~17.5K). **Value `3` was effectively absent in production** (contrary to the WW-510 bug report; observed as 1 order total, 19783360, and 0 rows on 2026-09-11; `3` is a Laravel-side errored/retry state on other flags). Stuck orders were usually `2`.

#### `items.odoo_sync` (legacy) and pending-inventory deduction

⚠️ Odoo-era `items.odoo_sync`: `0`=not synced, `1`=syncing, `2`=synced (~14M), `5`=unknown (~22K) — now a `(deleted)` column on `laravel_live`. **Orders not yet imported into the ERP must be deducted from available inventory** when deciding whether to disable a product; post-cutover the `receiver_product_inventory` view does this (sellable = available + `prelist_qty`; unsynced demand = parent `ec_order.serp_sync IN (0,2)` over 30 days; `reservation_hold_qty` from `inventory_reservations`). Raw on-hand alone never reflected availability.

#### Shipping label, not the production slip, finalizes inventory

⚠️ Printing a production slip does **NOT** mark an order shipped or finalize inventory — **ShipStation LABEL generation** does. "Reserved" should equal unshipped in ShipStation. Labels generate near-instantly on a recipient order, so there's a tiny window to fix negative-reserved before shipment (which is exactly why the negative-inventory bug bites).

#### `sw_fulfill` is in-house-vs-vendor, not shipment status

⚠️ **AI guesses** `sw_fulfill` means shipping-label/shipment status.
✅ **Reality:** It's the in-house-vs-vendor flag: `1`=SugarWish's own fulfillment center assembles & ships; `0`=vendor drop-ships (`vendor_id` on receiver_products, `vendor_order_number` on ec_order); `NULL`=legacy/unknown (~74%).

#### `ec_order.size` is a `buyer_products.id`, not a size

⚠️ `ec_order.size` is **MISNAMED** — it holds **`buyer_products.id`**, not a physical size. Join: `ec_order.product_type = buyer_products.product_type AND ec_order.size = buyer_products.id`. Real size is `buyer_products.size_name_id`. **For GC orders this collides** with disabled `receiver_products` (low buyer_product ids overlap; affected values include 41, 61–65, 90–91, 116, 137, 353, 923) — custom-shop slips read the wrong candy SKU. Fix: read the `items` table, not `receiver_products` by `ec_order.size`. **There is NO `recipient_email` column on `ec_order`** — querying it errors; sender/receiver email comes from `giftcards_card`. `receiver_orders` is NOT the shipment table (`ec_order` is). `buyer_orders.product_sku` is free-text — use `ec_order` for attribution. `ec_order` triggers have huge blast radius (Munyr can disable).

#### `giftcards_card` ↔ `ec_order` is one-to-MANY; join on `card_id`

⚠️ `ec_order.giftcards_card_id = giftcards_card.**card_id**` (NOT `giftcards_card.id`). One gift fans out to MANY shipments via reships (one card observed with **13,719** ec_order rows). `ec_order.increment_id` is the order number sent to the ERP. Chain: `giftcards_card → ec_order (increment_id) → sale_order`. `ec_order` "pending" status is ~99% (= giftcard issued, not redeemed) — **NOT** "awaiting payment."

#### `ec_order` has TWO id columns

⚠️ `ec_order.order_id` (short numeric, the `component_orders.order_id` join key) ≠ `ec_order.increment_id` (public-facing, e.g. `20019722440`, the `sale_id` / `serp_stock_picking.sale_id`). Join `component_orders` on `order_id`; chain to the ERP on `increment_id`.

#### Orders marked synced BEFORE confirmation (Odoo-era, no transactional integrity)

⚠️ The Odoo-era order sync had **no transactional integrity** — orders were marked `oddo_synchronized=1` **before** Odoo confirmed. Dec 23, 2024: **906 ec_orders marked synced but never sent to Odoo** (ghosts). Fix required marking synced **only after** success. There is **no `odoo_id` stored on `ec_order`** — matching relied on `increment_id` / `order_id`. The SERP-era order queue does NOT repeat this: `flush_claims` (`workers/order_processing.py`) writes the `serp_sync` claims as one Laravel transaction per chunk only **after** every SERP mirror in that chunk has committed.

#### Duplicate charges & orders

⚠️ Stripe charge with no order / duplicate charge = known race (WW-798, WW-1085); the daily detection n8n job has silently failed without alerts. Checkout add-to-cart race: a hardcoded **2-second `setTimeout`** between `/buyer/recipient-info` and `/buyer/add-to-cart` silently drops the cart item if the session write is slow. Duplicate orders are frequently **backend system-generated** (queue/dispatch re-fires), not double-clicks — check `order_queue_batches`/dispatch logs (one order shipped 24× in ShipStation).

---

### Inventory Structural Problems

- **Single-location limitation (Odoo era):** Laravel could only pull available inventory from ONE Odoo stock location; nearly all SKUs were stored under `ew/stock/fulfillment` even when physically in TY — Odoo could not distinguish EW vs TY. Sellable stock now reaches Laravel via `serp_published_stock`, which has **no location column** (one row per product: `on_hand_qty`, `reserved_qty`, `unreserved_outbound_qty`) — finished-goods on-hand is summed over internal locations flagged `serp_stock_location.is_fulfillment = 1` (components over all internal locations), so it still cannot tell EW from TY stock.
- **Off-site vendor stock is invisible** (vendors hold cartons/slips/stickers, ship in as POs land; Mike infers by subtraction). **No inventory snapshot-in-time mechanism** — cannot prove systems matched at a point in time, complicating reconciliation. **Leadership distrusted Odoo inventory** (default: the system is wrong, not the forecast).
- **Outer-shipper stockouts** (corrugated mailing boxes: 2/4/8/12-pick, grand, mini) HOLD shopped orders until a pallet arrives.
- **Negative inventory / oversell:** Laravel holds qty for orders not yet imported into the ERP → the ERP never reserves/subtracts → caches stagnant; when an order ships fast, inventory never deducted. Team resolves "reserved negatives" manually daily; Odoo-era workaround: nudge on-hand by 1, apply, revert.

---

### Performance Footguns (the ones that time out)

#### Negated predicates on multi-million-row tables

⚠️ **CRITICAL:** Never filter `state` with a **negated predicate** (`NOT IN` / `!=` / `<>`) on huge tables — `stock_move` / `serp_stock_move` (~14.5–15.8M rows), `stock_move_line` (~14.5M), `stock_valuation_layer` (~13.4M). It forces a seq scan and blows the **Odoo.sh 330s statement timeout** (and the 30s db-MCP timeout on `serp_app`). Use a **positive IN-list** with all states: `state IN ('draft','confirmed','waiting','partially_available','assigned')`. (Odoo timeouts were usually **external n8n load** — e.g. the "Disable Unreserved Products V2"/oversell workflow `VRdmXlm2XTeRbOyT` re-firing a 330s query with no backoff; that workflow is now **disabled** ("Disabled During SERP Cutover"). Check `odoo.log` for "reaching the timeout limit of 330.0 seconds" when reading frozen Odoo.)

#### `sa_projections` / forecast query is a 7–10 minute cartesian bomb

⚠️ `sa_projections.sql` is 437 lines / 15–20+ CTEs and processes ~3.3M intermediate rows (a **CROSS JOIN** of `week_settings` × `final_sku_popularity`) to emit ~210K–492K rows; takes **7–10 minutes** — far past the 30s API timeout (later raised to 120s). It also **ignores** its `current_skus` parameter and always returns ALL projections. Fixes: pre-compute nightly / materialize CTEs / add composite indexes (`product_type_key_sizes(product_type, size_name)`, `sku_product_type_key(sku, product_type)`, `size_projections_copy(year, week, product_type)`). Missing index on `mrp_bom_line(bom_id)` is also CRITICAL.

#### Forecast USED to have two orchestration paths that drifted (fixed 2026-05-26)

⚠️ Dashboard `/api/forecast` uses `SupplierForecastPipeline`; the CSV export used to call the legacy `forecast_service.generate_forecast()`, so **fixes didn't propagate between them** (e.g. the `date_utils.py` Mode-4 W52/year-boundary fix landed in the pipeline but not legacy export → CSV showed zeros at the W52→2027 cliff while the dashboard was correct). Fixed in commit `07ea8b737` (2026-05-26): `routers/forecast/export.py` now runs the same `SupplierForecastPipeline`, and `generate_forecast` no longer exists — if an old note blames "legacy export", it's history. Also: `utils/forecast_cache.py`'s docstring points at non-existent files; the real impl is the `cache/` package. When a forecast number is right in one place and wrong in another, suspect pipeline-vs-legacy divergence.

#### Forecast data sources, metrics & throughput

⚠️ Projections always read from **`size_projections_copy`** (not `size_projections`). `product_type_key` (PTK) = `'ProductType|Size'`; dashboard and `sa_projections.sql` MUST produce identical PTK strings. Three non-interchangeable "days of inventory" metrics: SA Days / Total Days (`/forecast/live-products`, last-7-day basis), supplier `total_inventory` (`/forecast/dashboard`, 25-week forecast), ecard days-to-90% (`/forecast/ecard-inventory`, simulation). **`/forecast/throughput`** (`backend/services/fulfillment_throughput.py`) = per-day **production/packing** counts, bucketed by `DATE(COALESCE(mp.date_finished, write_date, date_start, create_date))` — effectively `DATE(date_finished)` for any done MO. It **read live Odoo pre-cutover**; it now reads the `serp_*` tables (`packing_source="serp"`), bucketing days in UTC via `CONVERT_TZ` to reproduce Odoo's calendar-day boundaries. Feeds a **per-period packing bonus** (`fulfillment_throughput_bonus.py`). Because `date_finished` defaults to when the MO is marked done (not when it was physically packed), late-entered slips land on the wrong day — see the `mrp_production` `date_finished` footgun.

#### Forecast TTLs and caching surprises

⚠️ `TTL_STATIC_DATA`=600s, `TTL_DYNAMIC_DATA`=300s, `TTL_VOLATILE_DATA`=120s (defined twice — `services/forecast/cache/core.py` and `services/forecast/pipeline/data_sources.py` — change both; the old "static and dynamic are both 600s" is stale). Only ~1 of ~18 cache keys is warmed (hit rate ~20%). There are **three** cache layers (SharedForecastDataCache, ResponseCache, CrudCache); `CostTrackerService` and `ComponentForecastService` **bypass** caching entirely. Forecast `Decimal` columns serialize to JSON **strings**, so frontend `.toFixed()` throws — converters must `float()` them.

#### Forecast zero-cliff at the 2027 year boundary

⚠️ `size_projections_copy` / `week_settings` (now in `serp_app`, not Retool) are extended only a few weeks at a time — live `size_projections_copy` reaches **2027 W5** (845 rows for 2027 vs 8,788 for 2026). Any forecast week past the last projected week INNER-JOINs to nothing → **ZERO demand** (originally observed as real through W52 12/20/2026 then exactly 0 for 2027). Check `MAX(year, week)` before trusting a far-horizon number; fix is to extend the projection tables forward or cap the horizon. "Missing/buggy data" is often just a limit/filter slice or stale cache, not a real gap.

#### Historical inventory reconstruction is O(all transactions)

⚠️ `stock_quant` stores **current state only**. Reconstructing historical inventory means scanning `stock_move_line` (8.8M+ rows before a cutoff) with 4-way JOINs → 60–90s timeouts. No single index fixes it; cache/pre-compute snapshots/limit date range instead.

#### FastAPI serialization

⚠️ `response_model` **silently strips** fields not in the Pydantic schema (SERP has `ForecastItem` vs `ForecastItemSchema` — fields added to one but not the other vanish). Raw-dict endpoints with NO `response_model` serialize `Decimal` as a JSON **string** → frontend `.toFixed()` throws; fix with `float()` at the converter.

#### Query-timeout & engine quirks

⚠️ The db MCP server enforces a **30s query timeout** (MySQL `SET SESSION max_execution_time=30000`; PostgreSQL `SET statement_timeout=30000` — **NOT** a startup parameter, that errors with "unsupported startup parameter"). MySQL `ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE` only updates the **first** unique index (don't use on multi-index tables); `INSERT IGNORE` swallows **all** errors, unlike PG `ON CONFLICT`. A 30s "timeout" on a TRIVIAL query is a connection problem, not a slow query.

⚠️ **A `serp_app` query that works in the `mysql` CLI can still fail in the app.** `serp_routed_pool` / `laravel_routed_pool` execute through SQLAlchemy `text()`, which escapes `%` itself — write a bare `%` (`DATE_FORMAT(x,'%Y-%m')`); a hand-written `%%` reaches MySQL literally. Raw-cursor pools (e.g. `pools/serp_live.py`) need `%%`. `text()` also parses any `:word` — even inside a SQL comment — as a bind parameter ("A value is required for bind parameter"). PG→MySQL ports trip on MySQL reserved words used as aliases (e.g. `ssl`). Smoke-test new `serp_app` SQL through the real pool, not the CLI.

⚠️ **Don't drop "dead" FK indexes on big `serp_*` tables.** The ORM's generic unlink (`_check_ondelete_constraints`) runs `UPDATE … SET fk = NULL WHERE fk IN (…)` for every set-null Many2one pointing at the deleted model; an index on a nearly-empty FK column looks unused but is what keeps that from being a full-table scan that locks every scanned row. Migration `20260916_212232_drop_tier_a_dead_indexes` dropped four such indexes; on 2026-09-28 a failed MO create's cleanup unlink scanned `serp_stock_move.created_production_id` (~9.7M rows) and `serp_stock_move_line.production_id` (~15.7M, never indexed) for ~240s, stalling the order queue. `20260928_192637_restore_unlink_fk_indexes` restored them (plus `created_purchase_line_id`, `serp_account_move.reversed_entry_id`, `serp_stock_valuation_layer.stock_landed_cost_id`). Serpy MO items now require `bom_id`, and a failed confirm no longer calls `mo.unlink()` (rollback cleans up).

#### SSH tunnels

⚠️ Remote prod DBs (live Laravel, WishDesk) are only reachable through a bastion-host SSH tunnel. `paramiko` removed `DSSKey` → switch the tunnel library to **ssh2 / ssh2-python**.

⚠️ **MySQL 8.0 `AUTO_INCREMENT` reads STALE from `information_schema.TABLES` / `SHOW TABLE STATUS` after an `ALTER TABLE … AUTO_INCREMENT = N`** (verified on serp_test/serp_app 8.0.46 + local 8.0.45). Those views CACHE the counter and only refresh after **`ANALYZE TABLE <t>`** or an actual insert — a bare read right after an ALTER/seed shows the OLD value. Real case (2026-09-18): the SERP seeder's floor-bump (`WORKER_ID_GAP`, dropped 10M→1 in #1247 so SERP-native rows get small ids) set `serp_sale_order` to `odoo_max+1`, but `information_schema` still read **12,416,237** (= `odoo_max + 10,000,000` from a pre-#1247 run) while the real counter was **2,416,238** — confirmed by `ANALYZE TABLE` (metadata → 2,416,238) and the actual next-insert id. **Never diagnose a "stuck high auto-increment" from those views** — `ANALYZE TABLE` first (non-destructive, stats-only) or read a real insert's id. A `WHERE id >= <n>` row check tells you about DATA, not the counter. Also note: **`ALTER … AUTO_INCREMENT` CAN lower a counter in 8.0** (the "ALTER only raises" belief is false here — proven: raise→truncate→reload→`ALTER=max+1` yields the small next id).

---

### ID Strategy & Join Footguns

#### Odoo↔Laravel IDs are FABRICATED, not foreign keys

⚠️ **`components.odoo_id` is SYNTHETIC = `'800' + components.id`** (varchar), NOT a real `product_product.id`. `buyer_products.odoo_id = '500' + buyer_products.id`. Joining either directly to Odoo PKs **matches the wrong rows**. Real Odoo PK is a different range (1789–29210). The real bridge is **`product_template.sugarwish_id`** (matched by SKU = `product_product.default_code`). Failed-products email "800611" → component 611 → real Odoo product via `sugarwish_id`.

#### `sugarwish_id` prefix decoding

⚠️ `product_template.sugarwish_id` (lives on TEMPLATE, **not** `product_product`): prefix `'800'` → strip → `components.id`; prefix `'500'` → strip → `buyer_products.id`; other int → `receiver_products.product_id` OR `buyer_products.id`; `0`/`NULL` → no mapping (**~2,264 of 7,515** as of 2026-09-11; was ~1,371). Don't assume non-800 means receiver — could be buyer. `sugarwish-odoo` `sale.py` filters `startswith('500')`/`startswith('800')`. **`receiver_products.product_id`** is the SugarWish product id, **NOT** Odoo `product_id`.

#### NEVER join `serp_*` and Odoo tables on `id = id` — join on `odoo_id`

⚠️ Join SERP↔Odoo on the **`odoo_id`** column.

- Odoo-origin rows: `id = odoo_id` (`<1B`). SERP-runtime post-seed rows: small ids above `MAX(odoo_id)`, `odoo_id=NULL`. SERP-merge rows: `id >= 1B`, `odoo_id=NULL` (the seeder bumped AUTO_INCREMENT to 1B during seed, then reset to `MAX(odoo_id)+1` at finalize).
- The **durable** "SERP-native" signal is `odoo_id IS NULL` (`IS NOT NULL` = Odoo-sourced), **NOT** `id >= 1B` (old code filtering `id < 1B` broke after finalize).
- `id = odoo_id` holds **only** for Odoo-seeded rows; child rows (move/move_line/quant) left `odoo_id=NULL` until stamped. Post-cutover, new rows are SERP-native with `odoo_id` NULL by design. (`odoo_id_stamper` was retired 2026-06-25.)
- ⚠️ `isWorkerRow = (id != odoo_id)` misclassifies AUTO_INCREMENT-collision worker rows as seed rows. Better worker-row signal: `name = 'S' + ec_order_id`, or odoo_id origin — **not** `id != odoo_id`.

#### `serp_purchase_order_line` FK

⚠️ Earlier notes said `serp_purchase_order_line.order_id` referenced the pre-rename `purchase_order` table (pre-migration-008) and that inserts with a non-null `order_id` failed — that was a `laravel_local` artifact. **Live `serp_app` check: the only FK on `serp_purchase_order_line` is `order_id → serp_purchase_order`.** Check the FK on the specific local DB before assuming either way.

#### Stock-picking↔PO link is a string `origin` in Odoo, not an FK

⚠️ Odoo links picking→PO via `stock_picking.origin = 'P01906'` (text), **not** an FK. SERP extends with a real `purchase_order_id` FK. Don't assume a relational constraint in Odoo.

#### SKU is NOT unique; SKU/name fields are sometimes swapped

⚠️ **13 component SKUs are duplicated across multiple ids** (use `component.id` for linking, not SKU). **`receiver_products` SKU `SA-01-026-A` exists twice** (product_ids 1706 & 4499 — one deleted). **58 components have SKU and name swapped** (e.g. `sku="Halloween-XLARGE-Candy..."`, `name="B-AAA-HAL-22-001-XLG"`) — Halloween/Winter/Valentine seasonal rows. Code detects via `looks_like_component_sku()` and fixes/dedupes. ~483 `product_product` rows have **NULL `default_code`** and must be excluded from sync (`WHERE default_code IS NOT NULL`).

#### Alias shared-name columns

⚠️ `write_date`, `create_date` etc. are ambiguous on multi-table SERP joins — table-qualify up front.

---

### Inventory, Stock & Valuation

#### Negative inventory is mostly normal — but watch internal locations

⚠️ ~482–5,918 quants carry negative quantities (totaling tens of millions of units). Most are in **virtual** locations (Vendors/Production/Inventory-adjustment) where negatives are normal double-entry accounting. **Only negatives in INTERNAL locations** (Stock/Fulfillment) are real problems. A cycle count does **not** drop reservations — `quantity=0` with `reserved_quantity>0` yields negative available (root cause of negative West Coast Qty in Laravel).

#### Only `usage='internal'` is real inventory; `inventory_date` filter is wrong

⚠️ Only `stock_location.usage='internal'` holds real stock. Outbound moves go to virtual Customers (id 5), inbound from virtual Vendors (id 4). Several SQL files historically filtered `sq.inventory_date IS NOT NULL` to "find real stock" — that's **fragile and wrong** (it's the scheduled-count field). Use `usage='internal'`.

#### Only `state='done'` moves change inventory; `quantity_done` is computed

⚠️ Only `stock_move.state='done'` actually changes inventory (`assigned` = reserved/ready-to-pick, NOT shipped). In SERP, `quantity_done` is a **computed, store=False** field summed from `stock_move_line.qty_done` — ⚠️ **but it DOES have an inverse**: `_set_quantity_done` (`stock_move.py:155-205`, mirroring Odoo's `_quantity_done_set`) distributes a written value across move lines and creates one if none exist, and is used in production (`mrp_unbuild_actions.py:236`). The old "writing it silently no-ops (no inverse method)" is stale. The misleading error "Set quantity_done on at least one move" still means no line carries a `qty_done`. Editing `stock_move_line.qty_done` remains the **safer explicit** route — the inverse deliberately creates lines without `product_uom_qty` (`stock_move.py:181-190`, Odoo L350-353: copying it "used to plant a phantom reservation"). `_action_done` historically used `product_uom_qty` (ordered) instead of actual completed qty (a bug).

#### 48,909 stuck stock_moves block allocation (frozen Odoo data)

⚠️ **48,909 stock_moves stuck in `assigned`/`confirmed`/`waiting`** with active `sale_line_id` in Odoo (27,044 assigned + 8,842 confirmed in 2026; ~15K from pre-2024, oldest May 2023). Top product `B-CCC-REG-22-MUG-XXX` ~2,404 stuck moves. They permanently locked `reserved_quantity`. **Odoo's `do_unreserve()` is buggy:** when `qty_done > 0` it sets `product_uom_qty=0` but **skips `_recompute_state()`**, leaving "zombie" moves stuck in `assigned`. **NEVER unreserve via direct SQL** — it desyncs `stock_move_line.reserved_qty` from `stock_quant.reserved_quantity` and triggers "Cannot unreserve more than in stock." Must use `stock.move._do_unreserve()` / `stock.picking.do_unreserve()`. Cleanup script: `scripts/odoo_cleanup_stuck_moves.py`. (Frozen Odoo prod has the server actions: `ir_act_server` id 376 "Unreserve" (`stock.picking`), id 426 "Unreserve" (`mrp.production`), id 617 "Correct inconsistencies for reservation" — all `state='code'`.) The same shell-repair rule applies in SERP: use workflow methods, not quant-only corrections.

#### SVL FIFO order, `sequence_number`, landed cost

⚠️ `stock_valuation_layer` has **NO `sequence_number` column** (nor does the SERP mirror) — FIFO order is by **layer id / `create_date` ascending**. Value **sign** is source of truth (positive=in, negative=out); `remaining_qty`/`remaining_value` only meaningful on positive inbound layers (~3,705 with `remaining_qty>0` = current inventory $ in frozen Odoo). **Landed cost adds VALUE only** — it does NOT create on-hand quantity; it changes `unit_cost`/`value`, not `stock_quant.quantity`. Common split method in prod was `by_current_cost_price` (54 cases), not equal.

#### `_gather()` DOES expand child locations (SERP) — and a parent→child transfer self-cancels

⚠️ **Corrected 2026-09-11 — an older entry said the opposite and was stale.** It claimed SERP's `_gather()` used an exact `location_id` match "while Odoo uses `child_of`", framed as an intentional divergence. That predates the "Bug #4" fix and is **wrong**. Verified in `backend/serp_orm/models/stock_quant.py` (re-checked on `origin/main`, `strict` param at L109/L134): when `strict=False` (**the default**) SERP expands the location to all descendants via `get_child_locations` / `parent_path`, with the comment `Odoo L506: child_of expansion`. `strict=True` is the exact-match branch (`Odoo L510`). So child-bin stock IS visible to a parent-location caller.

⚠️ **FIXED 2026-09-11 (SERP PR #1140, merged to `dev`, on `main`) — the consequence below is history for the Serpy/Products-page transfer path.** The fix did NOT change the ORM: the transfer handler (`workers/handlers/serp_orm/stock.py::_pre_reserve_at_move_source`) now reserves each new move **strictly at its own source** and creates the line before `action_assign`, so the non-strict gather never runs — which is what Odoo 15 effectively does. The ORM's `_action_assign` still calls `_update_reserved_quantity` non-strict (`stock_move_actions.py` ~L865), so any **new** caller that reserves at a parent location without pre-reserving re-opens the trap. The PR also flagged a mirror-image bug in inventory adjustments (non-strict baseline read vs exact write), addressed the same day by `6d12fb787` ("read the bin you write in inventory adjustment and location move").

⚠️ **The original consequence: a parent→child internal transfer silently self-cancelled, inflating inventory.** `stock_move_actions.py` (~L726 at the time of the finding) calls `_update_reserved_quantity` **without passing `strict`**, so it defaults to `False` and the source gather expands _into the destination subtree_ (line numbers drift; re-grep `_update_reserved_quantity`). The move line is then stamped at the gathered quant's bin ("The gathered quant's bin, NOT self.location_id") while `location_dest_id` keeps the move's dest, and `_action_done` decrements at the line's bin (`decrement_loc = ml_loc`). On a parent→child move both ends resolve to the **same** child bin: debit and credit cancel, the parent is never debited, and a phantom unit is left behind.

Neither field is individually wrong — **the corruption is emergent**, which is why it survived review. The trigger is **direction, not entry point**: verified 2026-09-11 on `serp_test` with product 29324 (`SA-51-042-E`), where `EW/Stock` (8) → `EW/Stock/Fulfillment` (2008, a child of 8) failed identically from **two different front doors** — the Products page UI (draft #3005, move `EW/INT/05200`) and SERPY (draft #3006, move `EW/INT/05201`). Both produced a move line with `location_id = location_dest_id = 2008`. The reverse direction (child→parent, draft #3004) worked, because 2008 is a leaf with no descendants to expand into. The phantom `1.0000` was still sitting on quant `10166775` at `EW/Stock` (write_date 12:31:47), untouched by the later transfers.

PR #1140's audit found every other `_gather`/reservation caller already passes `strict=True`, and checked live Odoo: its internal transfers create the move line at the move's own source before `action_assign` (126 done parent→child transfers since 2026-06-01, all 133 lines kept the PARENT as `location_id`), so Odoo never gathers in this case. **Practical rule:** pass `strict=True` when the operation must not pull from child bins — `stock.scrap.action_validate` already does (`stock_scrap.py`, "scrap is AT a specific location, not 'this or descendants'"). Before trusting any "SERP doesn't expand child locations" claim, re-read `_gather` directly (line numbers drift).

#### Negative inventory floored to 0 on the Odoo→Laravel sync (dead)

⚠️ The Odoo→Laravel inventory sync **floored negatives to 0** (`sugarwish_stock.py` lines 75–77), hiding backorder depth and keeping oversold products enabled; the proposed fix sent `(available − unreserved demand)`. With Odoo frozen the `SynchronizeOdooInventory` sync is **dead** — sellable qty now comes from `serp_published_stock` (which upserts zeros and preserves sign). Relevant when reading pre-cutover data.

#### `odoo_inventory` was stale; the old daily report used the wrong column AND wrong location (columns now deleted)

⚠️ `receiver_products.odoo_inventory` (now `(deleted)`) was a stale cache (76–86% of records >30 days old) — pre-cutover. The Daily Operations inventory report wrongly read `odoo_inventory` instead of `inventory_qty` (off by up to **180×**: e.g. `inventory_qty=1,974` vs `odoo_inventory=11`) AND only summed the **Fulfillment** location, missing Stock/Production/etc. — massively underreporting (SA-03-050-A real Fulfillment 1,732, report showed 11). Both `odoo_inventory` and `inventory_qty` are now `(deleted)` — use the `receiver_product_inventory` view.

#### Inventory counts live in Retool, verified by 2-user agreement

⚠️ Physical counts used to live in **Retool** (`serp_inventory_counts` / `_live`), NOT Odoo or manage. They now live in **`serp_physical_counts`** in `serp_app` (prod) / `serp_test` (staging) — `settings.INVENTORY_COUNTS_TABLE`, env = DB, no suffix; vendor counts in `serp_vendor_inventory_counts`; `serp_inventory_counts_backup` / `_live_backup` in `serp_app` are leftovers. A count is **VERIFIED** only when **2+ different users** record the same `sku + location_id + fulfillment_entry_index` with matching quantity (single count = unverified; **vendor counts do NOT satisfy** the 2-user rule). `fulfillment_entry_index` is NULL for ~75% of records (general stock). Uncounted SKUs are **zeroed on sync** by design. Test location IDs **2001/2002** appeared in the old `serp_inventory_counts` but **don't exist** in `stock_location` — sync validation now blocks them.

#### `qty_received` / `qty_invoiced` DO auto-update in SERP — imperatively, not via `@api.depends`

⚠️ **Corrected 2026-09-11 — an older entry said these were "static columns that never update". Both halves are false.** `qty_received` is recomputed inside `stock.move._action_done` (`stock_move_actions.py`, ~L1510-1520, compute ~L1728) for incoming receipts **and** refundable purchase returns (which subtract); `qty_invoiced` is recomputed by `account.move._update_qty_invoiced` (`account_move_billing.py`, ~L803/L843) called from `action_post`/`button_cancel`/`button_draft` (`account_move_actions.py`). **The real caveat:** SERP has no `@api.depends` machinery, so these are **imperative calls at the state transition** — a write that bypasses those methods will NOT refresh the columns. In **Odoo** they are stored computes. (Odoo data quality: 20 over-received lines where `qty_received > product_qty`, e.g. P01654 +30K; 266 partially received; 10 cancelled POs with 2–18 active moves.)

#### PO date fields: use `effective_date` for arrival

⚠️ **Three distinct PO dates:** `date_order` = CREATED (not arrival), `date_planned` = expected (user guess), **`effective_date` = ACTUAL arrival** (NULL until the receipt picking is validated `state='done'`). **Use `effective_date` for inventory variance** — using `date_order` makes variance reports wrong by months. PO `state='purchase'` means **approved**, not received; `invoice_status='invoiced'` ("Fully Billed") means received-so-far qty is billed, **not** that the whole PO is settled (a partially-received PO with an open backorder still reads `invoiced`). The `serp_approval_thresholds` design (ops=$5K, finance=$50K) was never wired — the table does **not exist** in `serp_app` and no backend code references it — and PO gate columns (`ready_for_approval_at`, `ops_approved_at`, …) do not exist in `serp_app` — design intent only. **Serpy approvals are actually enforced by `config.py`:** `SERPY_APPROVAL_RULES` (`po_receiving` + `po_creation` → James Emeric only) and `SERPY_THRESHOLD_APPROVAL_RULES` (inventory adjustments/scraps netting > ±500 units → Matthew Patrick, no admin bypass). The live PO email sender is NOT yet enabled on live SERP — James used normal email for Q4 POs (9/28).

#### `product_qty=0` BOM lines cause division-by-zero

⚠️ **~30–48 active BOM lines have `product_qty=0`** (optional/conditional packaging — labels/tape), causing "division by zero" in RM→SA conversions and `inventory_variance.py`. **Always filter `product_qty > 0`** before using it as a denominator. (~437 lines also have qty=0 in `mrp_bom_line`.)

#### BOM conversion rate must exclude cancelled moves

⚠️ BOM conversion rate (`mrp_production.product_qty / SUM(mrp_bom_line.product_qty)`) breaks if cancelled moves are included — SA-45-008-A showed **27.17×** instead of 1.0 because 2,500 cancelled units were counted. **Filter `state='done'`.**

#### Archived products still hold stock & reservations

⚠️ Archived variants (`product_product.active=false`) **retain `stock_quant` and `assigned` reservations** even while the template is active. Filter `pp.active=true` for LIVE inventory but **NOT** when auditing history. **Archive status did NOT sync from Laravel to Odoo** — an RM archived in Laravel (`receiver_products.archive=1`) could remain `active=true` in Odoo, so it kept appearing in forecasts (check the same on `serp_app`). `rm_inventory.sql` and `packaging_component_inventory.sql` are **MISSING** the `pp.active=true` filter (`sa_inventory.sql` has it correctly).

### Kits, BOMs & Phantom Explosion

#### THREE parallel kit/BOM systems with colliding id namespaces

⚠️ **AI assumes ONE unified kit/BOM system.** There are **three**, drifting independently with **separate, numerically-colliding id namespaces**:

1. **Laravel `component_kits`** (box SKUs `B-*`, `S-*`) — `buyer_products.default_kit`/`seasonal_kit` → `kits.id`; archived lines are flagged by `components.hide` (1=archived, 0=active); ⚠️ `component_kits` is **frozen** post-cutover and the old `laravel_update_kit` op was removed 2026-09-09. Pure box SKUs MUST use Laravel kits.
2. **`serp_mrp_bom` phantom BOMs** (shippers `S-*`, inserts `I-*`, production paper `P-*`, cups `E-*`/`C-*`, merch) — edited via the `update_kit` op (legacy alias `serp_update_kit`) / "Update SERP Kit". The kit copy in **`laravel_live.serp_mrp_bom`** is the intended SoT. It began as a **one-time April-2026 snapshot** (~224 phantom rows) and is now actively edited: live it holds **439 rows, all `type='phantom'`, all `odoo_id` NULL, 253 `active=1`**, `create_date` 2026-04-08 → 2026-09-22 (the same 439 phantoms are mirrored into `serp_app.serp_mrp_bom`).
3. **Odoo `mrp_bom`** (manufacturing recipes; frozen), whose successor is `serp_app.serp_mrp_bom` normal BOMs.

⚠️ **ID namespace trap:** `kits.id` and `serp_mrp_bom.id` collide numerically (e.g. 682 is a `serp_mrp_bom` id, NOT a `kits.id`). `serp_find_kits_by_sku` returns `serp_mrp_bom` ids; passing one as `kit_id` to `laravel_update_kit` → all ops fail "not found"/"already exists" (live failure: draft #860, kit_ids 682/683/684). The `archive_impact_check` rule must call `find_laravel_kits` for the Laravel arm and `serp_find_kits_by_sku` only for the SERP arm.

⚠️ Laravel kits and phantom BOMs are **ADDITIVE, not overlapping** (Carolyn confirmed): "Kits in Odoo are used for everything but the colored box." Laravel = colored box only (89% of kits have exactly one `B-*` component); phantom = shippers/inserts/labels/contents. **Double-counting happens when a component (e.g. a soap dish) exists in both** — both sources must merge correctly. To replace/discontinue a component, fan the change across all three (emit `remove_component(old)`+`add_component(new)`; for Laravel set `hide=1` old / `hide=0` new).

#### Phantom BOMs don't manufacture — and aren't all live

⚠️ `type='phantom'` BOMs do NOT create manufacturing orders (zero `mrp_production` rows reference a phantom `bom_id`) — they **explode into delivery-order component moves at SALE time**, not MO time. **~96-98% of Odoo BOMs have `product_id=NULL`** (linked via `product_tmpl_id` only) — ⚠️ **SERP's `_bom_find()` handles this** (corrected 2026-09-11): `mrp_bom.py:170-219` mirrors Odoo 15's combined product-specific-OR-template-level domain, two-pass so the product-specific BOM wins. The old "no fallback, causing 'No active BOM found'" claim predates the 2026-05-28 rewrite. `/boms` must filter `type='normal'`; `/kits` filters `type='phantom'`. **Safe to archive phantom BOMs in Odoo** — all SERP runtime paths explicitly exclude phantoms (`boms.py`, `manufacturing.py` raises, `expander/odoo.py` raises).

#### Kit explosion is scoped by SOURCE TABLE

⚠️ Delivery-path kit explosion is scoped to **`items`-origin (gift-selection) lines ONLY**; `component_orders` box/buyer-product kits must **NOT** explode (prevents double-booking). Legacy receiver/popcorn pickings aren't built via BOM explosion at all — every `stock.move` has `bom_line_id=NULL` and a populated `sale_line_id` (flat 1:1), expanded at order-BUILD time, not via manufacturing BOMs. **April 2026:** BOM/kit expansion moved out of Odoo into Laravel/SERP (Jack, intentionally); Odoo received pre-expanded component items and buyer-product/packaging info **disappeared from Odoo sale orders** (~2026-04-16) — pull from **`component_orders`** in Laravel instead.

#### `serp_mrp_bom` seed gaps & FK nullability — CLOSED

⚠️ **Both halves corrected 2026-09-11 — these gaps are CLOSED.** (1) The "~62 of 121 phantom-BOM component product_ids missing from `serp_product_product` → BOM creation FK-fails" precondition is gone: verified **0 missing** of 149 distinct components on `serp_app` (and 0 of 69 on `serp_test`). (2) `serp_mrp_bom.product_id` is **NOT** `NOT NULL` — it is nullable (`mrp_bom.py:60` has no `required=True`; live column `nullable: true`; **1,402 of 1,675** rows on `serp_test` NULL at that time; live `serp_app` now 1,429 NULL of 1,913 — all of them among the 1,474 `normal` rows; the 439 phantoms all carry a `product_id`). Odoo BOM `product_id` being ~96-99% NULL (template-level) is still true, and SERP mirrors it.

---

### Popcorn, Wine & Product-Line Special Cases

#### Popcorn flavor is a $0 token; cost lives on the sticker

⚠️ **AI assumes** popcorn flavors map 1:1 to costed products or manufacture via BOM.
✅ **Reality:** A flavor SKU (`SA-02-017-A`) is a **$0 selection token created in LARAVEL only, ARCHIVED in Odoo**. The cost lives on the **City-Pop sticker** (`L-B02-REG-23-0NN-COX`) + 32oz cup. **Key trick:** the flavor's `receiver_products.product_id` **equals the sticker's `sugarwish_id`** (e.g. both 2082), so the order payload resolves onto the costed sticker, creating sticker SO lines, not bare popcorn. COGS bug: the SERP worker resolved by `product_sku` (bare $0 flavor) instead of `product_id` (sticker), undercounting ~$0.17–0.18/flavor — **fix is to mirror Odoo's `sugarwish_id` resolution (seed the map), NOT add a BOM or string-munge SKUs.** ⚠️ The historical SA-02 seed phantom-flip (`make_popcorn_boms_phantom()`) was **RETIRED 2026-09-01 (PR #1056)** — SA-02 BOMs now stay `normal` (matching Odoo), so popcorn no longer auto-explodes into cup+sticker leaves in the seed (reopening the COGS gap; resolution undecided). Popcorn costs for ~160 SKUs are **hardcoded** in `POPCORN_COSTS` (no BOM). **Popcorn orders should NOT carry merch/sleeve** (recurring City Pop bug WW-964). In forecast, popcorn is split CityPop vs Poppin and **explicitly NOT collapsed** (unlike other splits).

#### Wine is category-goal, churns SKUs, has no real BOM

⚠️ **AI treats** wine like fixed-SKU core products with per-SKU PO replenishment.
✅ **Reality:** Wine churns SKUs (opportunistic buys), should be **NON-core**, uses **category-level goals** (`operation_levels` keyed `"CategoryName|product_type_id"`, product_type 10 'Wine Tastings' & 14 'Wine'; exclude 25 '12 Nights', 550 'Vinebox'), fulfills at WCC (location_id 5), replenishes **outside ERP POs** (gravity racks, ~1-week lead). It has **no real BOM** — self-maps `SA-14→SA-14`, causing a Bulk-vs-Packed double-count of the same `stock_quant` (fix: zero out packed inventory for them). Brand-new wines correctly show **zero** weekly projections. Wrongly-core wine rows: 29 type-10 + 29 type-14 on 2026-06-01; live now **27 type-10 (Wine Tastings) still `sku_type='core'`, 0 type-14** — the type-10 cleanup is still open. Wine SERP only tracks tasting-kit categories {55, 56, 57} — **missing 165 (12-pack) and 190 (4-pack)**. Wine size ratios are hardcoded but `shipped_items_for_avg_cost.sql` returns no `size_tier`, so all wine defaults to the Large ratio (0.125).

### Branding, Sleeves, Merch & Print

#### Sleeve matching: EXTEND the existing entry, don't create new

⚠️ **AI creates a new branding entry** (or assumes each product gets its own).
✅ **Reality:** Sleeves match `ec_order.size` (a `buyer_products.id`) against `branding_records.physical_branding.entries[].buyer_product_ids[]`, keyed by **box family** (`a_small`, `a_medium`, `c_medium`, …). Multiple products in the SAME box **share ONE entry** (e.g. `a_small` covers Dog 42, Candy 6978, Popcorn 62). "Missing sleeve" = `buyer_product_id` absent from the entry's array → **fix is to ADD the id to the existing entry**, not create a new one. The admin `render-at-size` button copies the source entry's `buyer_product_ids` **verbatim** (wrong) → ghost entries that never match real orders; it should call `filterProductsForBox()`.

#### Two box-SKU vocabularies — do NOT cross-map

⚠️ RECIPE boxes (`merchandise_packaging_recipes` / `recipe_snapshot.outer_box_sku`): `c_1`, `c_1.25`, `c_1.5`, `c_2`, `c_3`, `c_4`. LIVERY/SLEEVE boxes (`branding_records.physical_branding`): `a_mini`, `a_small`, `a_medium`, `a_large`, `a_xlarge`, `c_1`, `c_small`, `h_small`. (`c_1` ≠ `c_small` — a real bug class.)

#### 5 distinct "no sleeve / wrong sleeve" root causes

⚠️ (a) `buyer_product_id` duplicated across two box entries → 2 sleeves; (b) `box_sku` not in `SKU_TRIM_TABLE` (e.g. `h_*`) → `normalizeSkuKey` throws → whole PDF aborts; (c) missing size entry; (d) NULL `physical_branding`; (e) `design_box_override`-only ghost rows.

#### Hot Sauce "2 sleeves" over-print

⚠️ `design_boxes` `a_medium`/`a_mini`/`a_xlarge` still list `product_type_ids=[6,46,49]` (includes 46/Hot Sauce) while `a_small`/`a_large` were cleaned to `[6,49]`. A Hot Sauce Medium buyer_product (type 46, size 3) matches BOTH `a_medium` AND `h_medium` → two entries with the same `buyer_product_id` → Livery prints 2 sleeves. Fix: `UPDATE design_boxes SET product_type_ids=JSON_ARRAY(6,49) WHERE sku IN('a_mini','a_medium','a_xlarge')`. Same shape on Gourmet Goods Medium (7026, `c_1.25`/`c_1.5`).

#### Removing a sleeve isn't enough — swap the eCard/boxcard too

⚠️ NULLing `branding_records` (physical/digital_branding=NULL, approvals=0, `print_render_status='not_required'`) does NOT fully fix a bad sleeve order — the **eCard/boxcard design on the giftcards card must ALSO be swapped and the slip regenerated** (esp. if already redeemed). WW-841: a logo-source filter applied to only 1 of 8 endpoints shipped a placeholder.

#### Custom mug print reads `ec_order.merchandise_selections`, not `branding_records`

⚠️ SWOP/Livery reads the mug image from **`ec_order.merchandise_selections.items[N].design_selected.print_image_url`** (matched by `item_id` to `branding_records.merchandise.items[N]`), **NOT** `branding_records.merchandise.items[0].designs[0]…`. Patching `branding_records` alone is NOT enough — `ec_order.design_selected` must be populated (redemption can write a fresh ec_order with `design_selected=null`, and then the PDF is not fixed). Custom Mug & Treats (bp 6950→6961) is **excluded from physical_branding generation** entirely, so every sleeve-eligible mug order mismatches at redemption.

#### Box card / insert nullness silently blocks printing

⚠️ `ecard_design.inserts` drives the printed note card. `inserts=NULL` or a missing `ecard_designs` row **breaks the PDF cron** (`is_printed=2`) and silently prevents the order from printing/shipping.

#### Print files silently fall back to low-res; Livery caches PDFs

⚠️ Print files are NOT always high-res. Silent fallbacks: render cron (`ENABLE_BRANDING_RENDER_CRON`) never set in live `.env` → `print_url=null` → falls back to a **50 DPI JPEG thumbnail** (`s3_url`; Livery: `renderUrl = render.print_url || render.s3_url`); `findPendingRecords()` once paginated `LIMIT 10` and missed newer branding; mug preview is **225×225** vs real **800×800** print (`PRINT_IMAGE_SIZE`) so the printer pulls the pixelated preview; slips can show low-res after Creative re-uploads. **Mug print is hard-capped at 800×800 everywhere** while sleeves got a high-DPI (`render_tier:'print'`, 1200+ DPI) path — mugs never did.

**Fixing data in SWAC does NOT regenerate Livery's cached PDFs** — they're cached at `{batch}/{orderId}_sleeve.pdf` by orderId and never invalidated on branding edits. To pick up a fix: click **"Regenerate"** (`POST /reset-status/:orderId`), then re-run `generate-batch`. The dashboard cell falls back to `all[0]` (looks fine) but the print path throws "no sleeve entries." Production slip PDFs do NOT regenerate when CS uploads a replacement logo (only the `mugs-live` print file does) → slip shows old low-res image.

#### `physical_branding` entries are all equal — filter by order's size

⚠️ One `branding_record.physical_branding.entries[]` can have **2–7** entries (one per box-size variant from a multi-size proposal). There is **NO active/primary/status flag** — all entries are equal. The print pipeline MUST filter to the entry whose `buyer_product_ids` CONTAINS `ec_order.size`, or it sends ALL PDFs (e.g. order 19777198 got 3). `print_render_status='rendered'` is NOT a reliable health check — broken records with missing `print_image_url` still show `rendered`; check the JSON path directly.

#### Livery sleeve SKU table throws on unknown keys

⚠️ Livery's `SKU_TRIM_TABLE` (`sleeve-pdf.js`) maps `box_sku`→trim spec. A missing key (e.g. `c_3` "Cube System Triple", or `h_*` hot-sauce keys) makes `normalizeSkuKey` **throw**, aborting the whole PDF silently (`ok:true` returned, no sleeve PDF written; stuck rows with `print_url=NULL`). Fix = add the trim spec.

### Serpy (AI Ops Agent)

#### "x/y synced" partial — now means SERP-side rejection

⚠️ A Serpy draft showing "x/y synced" (e.g. "Draft #683: 2/3 synced") means **x ops were applied, y = total ops; the partial count = validation REJECTED some ops** — it is NOT a SERP crash. **Pre-cutover** the target was Odoo and a partial meant *Odoo-side* validation rejected ops (the old reading, "2/3 synced to Odoo", "it's not a SERP failure"). **Since the cutover "synced" means applied to SERP**, so a partial = SERP-side validation rejection. A draft's ops are claimed **strictly in order**, and identical ops in one draft no longer collapse into one (2026-09-26).

⚠️ **"Fully synced" + picking `done` but quants unchanged = a putaway rule sent the stock back to its own source bin.** Seen in Odoo (draft #3289: a `stock_putaway_rule` for RM-01-140-A redirected EW/Stock → EW/Stock/R1-8-BL, the transfer's source) and now possible in SERP, which applies putaway rules since 2026-09-27 (`serp_stock_putaway_rule`, loaded by migration `20260927_102445`). Signature: the move LINE's `location_dest_id == location_id` while the move header shows the intended destination. It's a per-product data condition, not a worker outage — check the putaway rules for the destination before debugging the queue.

#### Serpy is an AI tool, not a person, and not "SERP"

⚠️ Don't read SERPY Slack messages as written by a person, and don't assume SERPY is a typo for SERP. It's Jack's AI inventory-ops tool (Slack bot `U096P936NQ7`).

#### Product write-routing is hardcoded per op type

⚠️ **AI assumes** Serpy decides product write-routing at runtime per attribute.
✅ **Reality:** Routing is **hardcoded per op type**; the AI only chooses the op type. A single op can write the ERP + Laravel and keep IDs wired (pre-cutover: Odoo + SERP + Laravel; since 9/26 all ERP ops route to SERP `serp:<entity>` handlers). Provenance bounds (creation-path go-lives): `product_template` 2026-03-24, `create_product` 2026-04-13, `create_receiver_product_everywhere` 2026-05-05 — anything before its path's go-live **cannot** have been Serpy. `create_uid=55` (jack@sugarwish.com) is NOT a reliable Serpy signal (it's both Serpy writes AND Jack's manual edits) — confirm via the sync queue (`serpy_sync_queue`; historical rows in the frozen Retool `odoo_sync_queue_live`); `ir_model_data.module='__export__'` = manual CSV import, not Serpy.

#### Serpy guards are fact-triggered, not semantic search

⚠️ **AI designs** rule retrieval as semantic search over the user's message.
✅ **Reality:** Serpy's guards are **fact-triggered** to catch what the user fails to mention; embeddings may add examples but never gate rules.

#### "Replace SKU" = component swap, not archive+activate

⚠️ Serpy "Replace SKU X with Y" for a box SKU should mean a **kit COMPONENT SWAP** (remove old + add new across every kit), NOT archive-old + activate-new. Intent is only implicitly encoded in commit `34bd6da9`; Serpy sometimes mis-resolves.

#### Serpy images never reach the model; bulk creation trips loop detection

⚠️ Serpy transcribes images via an Opus vision pass **before** the agent loop (`transcribe.py`) — the agent only sees structured text, never image bytes (`parser.py` appends a `[image: path]` placeholder). `find_laravel_receiver_products` / `find_components` used to accept a **single `query` string** (no batching), so bulk product creation (~35 SKUs) forced 35+ sequential calls and tripped `loop_detected` (threshold=3). This is fixed: both tools now take a **`queries` list** for batch lookup (`serpy/tools/cross_system.py`), though the loop detector (`LOOP_DETECTION_THRESHOLD = 3`, `serpy/agent/loop.py`) plus an empty-lookup circuit breaker still stop repeated searches. Old notes said the approval flow had no per-op-type permission check and hardcoded `reason=None`; now `config.py` `SERPY_APPROVAL_RULES`/`SERPY_THRESHOLD_APPROVAL_RULES` gate specific op types, and rejections collect a reason through a Slack modal (`serpy_reject_reason`, stored in `serpy_drafts.rejection_reason`). Serpy's Laravel writes (`workers/handlers/laravel.py`) go through `laravel_routed_pool` — **live sugarwish in prod, manage on serp-test/dev** (the old "Serpy writes to manage, not prod" is stale). `sku_type`/`is_core` are already in `RECEIVER_PRODUCT_WRITABLE`, and the handler sets `is_core` to match `sku_type=='core'`. Validate a failed-MO payload's SKU→product_id→bom_id against the ERP before recreating — payload labels can be the bug.

#### PO-receipt handler silently drops unmatched lines

⚠️ Serpy's `_execute_po_receipt` silently drops any payload item whose `product_id` doesn't match an existing `stock.move.line` (happens when a PO line was zeroed and its move set to cancel). The `matched_count==0` guard only fires if EVERY item is unmatched, so a partial drop passes silently (`partial=False`, no Slack). Real example: PO P01982, RM-05-064-A 60 lbs vanished while the handler logged "12 of 13 matched, synced." `po_receipt` also threw false "Sync Failed" on a successful receive (socket timeout + retry found the picking already `done`) — fixed with `_already_received_result()`. Genuinely unguarded handlers (idempotency footguns on replay): `mrp_unbuild`, PO line edit/add, `stock_picking_correction`, `bom_change` remove_component. (Written against the Odoo-era handler.) The SERP handler `workers/handlers/serp_orm/purchasing.py::execute_po_receipt` **fixes the P01982 silent drop** ("BUG #2"): payload items with no matching move line get a new move + line on the receipt picking via `picking.add_move`, only `matched_count == 0` raises, and `_already_received_result` is carried over. The idempotency list for the other handlers has not been re-audited against the SERP handlers.

---

### Product Classification & Forecasting Keys

#### `is_core` ⇔ `sku_type=='core'`, enforced only in app code

⚠️ `receiver_products`: `sku_type` VARCHAR ∈ {`core`~398, `seasonal`~25, `legacy`~4,810}, default `legacy`; `is_core` tinyint. **Rule: `is_core` MUST equal `(sku_type=='core')`, enforced in APP CODE ONLY** (helper `core_flag_for_sku_type`) — **no DB trigger** (live today: 0 mismatches; the former exception, product_id 4177 `SA-46-033`, is now `legacy`/0). "core" is a CEO-driven commitment on ~220 specific SA SKUs, not an arbitrary flag. **There is no `make_for` column** — "make for" = toggle is_core/sku_type. Classification belongs only on PARENT SKUs (`parent_sku IS NULL`) and propagates parent→child only in the forecast READ pipeline (display), **never written to children**. When cloning a SKU to a new location variant, `drop_level`s do NOT auto-copy. `drop_level`=floor (disable at/below); `threshold`≈2× drop_level=alert.

#### Product line comes from Laravel `product_type`, NOT Odoo category

⚠️ **AI assumes** Odoo `product_category` determines product line.
✅ **Reality:** Source of truth is **`laravel_live.product_type`** (int) + the `SA-NN-` SKU prefix. Odoo categories scatter the same products across multiple categories (unreliable). A category/`receiver-product` `product_type` mismatch makes a product **invisible despite being enabled & stocked** (daily Retool + n8n alert ~7am MDT flags conflicts — n8n "Catch and post receiver product and category conflicts", active). Types: 1=Candy, 2=Popcorn, 3/45=Cookies/Brownies, 5=Snacks, 6=Dog Swag, 10=Wine Tastings, 14=Wine, 16/40=Candles/Spa, 19/20=Coffee/Tea, 25=12 Nights, 39=Gourmet Pantry, 49=Candy & Snacks, 51=Gourmet Goods & Spa, 550=Vinebox, 567=Bakery & Cafe.

#### `product_type` means different things in different tables

⚠️ `ec_order.product_type` classifies the **order context**; `receiver_products.product_type` classifies the **physical product**. The same cookie can be `567` (Bakery & Cafe) on one order and `47` (Custom Mug & Treats) on another. Route by `ec_order.product_type`. SA SKUs are context-dependent in cost reporting — a SKU may show blank for a product-type week even though it shipped under a different type. **The two legitimately disagree all the time** — of stacked legs shipped since Jan 2026, **327 of 1,236** have an item whose `product_type` differs from the order's, and the common pairs are all valid cross-line picks. So an item/order type mismatch is NOT by itself a bug.

⚠️ **Gift cards (type `43`) are the exception that DOES have to match** — of 6,895 gift-card-item orders since Jan 2025, **6,894** carry `ec_order.product_type = 43` (one outlier in 14 months). Five fields move together on a real gift-card order and must be judged as a set, never restamped individually: `product_type=43` · `size` = a GC `buyer_products.id` (921/923/924/928…) · `sw_fulfill=0` (vendor/Tango) · `gift_total_picks=0` · `items.odoo_sync=5` (pre-cutover; that column is now `odoo_sync(deleted)` — post-cutover the parent `ec_order.serp_sync=5` carries the vendor/special marker). The `-43-` segment in `SA-43-0NN-A` SKUs IS the product-type number. **`44` is a sibling gift-card type** — Laravel special-cases 43 and 44 together on the stacked leg; 43 is the main one. **Do NOT confuse with `13`** — `giftcards_card.product_type = 13` is "Customize a Sugarwish", the _sender-side_ card product (that's what WW-1409's 567→13 fix was about; different table, different meaning). A gift card as the SECOND gift of a stacked card (`is_stacked=2`, `product_type=43`) is a **routine, supported flow**, not corruption. Because type 43 has `bypass_odoo=1`, mis-stamping a gift-card order with a non-GC type (e.g. 567) sent it into the Odoo importer, which could never find it by `sugarwish_id` → permanent failed-products log + daily email loop (WW-1727). ⚠️ **Fixing such a row by setting `product_type=43` ALONE produces a shape matching neither pattern** (43 with a Bakery `size`, `sw_fulfill=1`, `odoo_sync=0`) and books a real Bakery sale as a gift-card sale in every report keyed on `ec_order.product_type` — either correct all five fields or leave it and annotate.

#### `product_type_key` (PTK) strings must match EXACTLY across systems

⚠️ The dashboard and `sa_projections.sql` must produce **IDENTICAL** `product_type_key` ("ProductType|Size") strings or demand won't roll up. Popcorn sub-brands ('City Pop'/'Poppin & Mixin') normalize to 'Popcorn'; Custom Mug 'mug' and 'treats' are NOT separate SKUs (separate `size_name` rows). `sku_product_type_key` has base SKUs AND `'<sku>-bakerycafe'` variants — the base maps to the WRONG type for Bakery & Cafe (root cause of the 47,062 Bakery & Cafe orders with item-level cost gaps). `rm_weekly_demand_cache` uses **plural** `product_type_keys` (ARRAY), not singular. Note: `sku_projections.product_type` is **int** while `size_projections`/mapping tables use **text** — same semantic, different type. Several types are broken (exist in `size_projections_copy` but have no `product_type_key_sizes` mapping): Wine|Small Wine, Gift Sets|Deluxe/Large/X-Large, Candles, Gourmet Goods & Spa (51 has 0 `size_projections_copy` rows despite 61 SKUs). SKUs with no popularity row get **zero demand for ALL weeks** (popularity comes from REAL survey data, not code — new SKUs are zero until surveys arrive).

#### `-A` vs `-E` suffix and EW/TY fulfillment routing

⚠️ `-A` = standard/Taylor (location_id 2); `-E` = branded Englewood (EW, location_id 1). Branded (sleeve/merch) orders must use `-E`; non-branded must use `-A` (an hourly n8n workflow flags mismatches). Forecast rolls `-A` and `-E` as the **SAME** product. "Move quantity -A to -E" = a PAIR of inventory adjustments, never a stock_transfer. Fulfillment overrides: Bakery & Cafe is normally TY but **OVERRIDES to EW if sleeve/custom merch**; Mini Popcorn moves from Poppin & Mixin to CityPop if sleeved; Custom Mug & Treats is normally TY but overrides to EW if sleeve/merch (causing split EW/TY shipments, double shipping, manual tracking emails). ⚠️ **The suffix is a CONSEQUENCE, not a decision — no code anywhere reads or writes `-A`/`-E` to route an order.** Routing resolves a _location id_ (`buyer_products.custom_location_id` for branded, `location_id` otherwise) and filters the catalog on `receiver_products.location_id`; branded orders get `-E` only because `-E` products happen to live on `location_id=1`. So "merch → `-E`" holds only while `custom_location_id` is populated — when it is NULL the filter is skipped and the receiver sees both suffixes. Forecast location attribution reads the FIRST item's `location_id`; physical mug production is in Taylor but forecast usage **rolls up to Englewood**. Only EW/TY are valid for production-slip printing (`validate_slip_rows` ALLOWED_LOCATIONS raises "Print blocked" otherwise). (Taylor location `id=2` was slated to be retired at cutover — all vinebox_states ship_from=1 — but live Laravel still has `locations` id=2 "Taylor" with ~60 enabled, unarchived `receiver_products` on `location_id=2` (301 total), and `-A` routing still resolves there; don't treat TY as gone.)

⚠️ **PARTLY FIXED (live-checked 2026-09-29): `buyer_products.custom_location_id` was NULL on `bakery-cafe-nano` (id 7030) and `onepick-brownies` (id 6994)** — all ten sibling Bakery & Cafe rows carry `custom_location_id=1`. **7030 now has `custom_location_id=1`** (fixed since the 2026-08-17 finding, when 1,041 branded Nano orders since 2026-06-03 had been offered the unfiltered catalog and mostly picked wrong `-A` SKUs, at `mi_inventory_qty=0` Taylor stock — a column now `(deleted)`). **6994 is still NULL** — it had no branded orders yet (latent, not firing). The fix is Laravel-side data (`UPDATE buyer_products SET custom_location_id=1 WHERE id=6994`) and is the Laravel team's write, not SERP's — `custom_location_id` appears in SERP only in `seeding/snapshots/manage_schema.json`, no SERP logic reads it. **No data-assumption covers this** — `shipping_buyer_product_location_resolves` guards only non-NULL `location_id`, and `active_e_parent_skus_are_englewood` guards the `-E` catalog rows; nothing watches the routing _input_.

---

### Equivalency Ratio & Cost Tracking

⚠️ **Cost-per-equivalent is `dollars / units` (A·B/Z), NOT `dollars·ratio / units·ratio`** (the ratio cancels out). Popcorn ~$1.80–3.00 with equiv 0.5 → ~$0.90–1.50 per equivalent. The equivalency ratio normalizes cost across product types for the executive scorecard: Candy=1.0 (baseline), Popcorn=0.5, Gourmet Pantry/Spa ~0.75 (Wine/Gift Sets TBD). Cost method (per Ric Marquis) is **current/recent PO price ÷ BOM conversion (from MOs) × equivalency ratio, weighted-averaged — NOT FIFO accounting cost.** SA cost lookup REQUIRES a recent Manufacturing Order (no MO in last 6 months → "-" cost, even if the SKU shows popularity). Popcorn (no MO/BOM) uses the hardcoded `POPCORN_COSTS` dict. "Material Cost Rate" (actual fulfillments) and "Summary" (forecast-weighted by SKU popularity) tabs differ by source → expect small mismatches ($1.36 vs $1.32 for Candy). Cocktail Mixer SKUs 344–357 have **zero** cost data (no supplier/SVL/PO; `list_price=1.00` placeholder).

---

### FIFO / COGS / Accounting

⚠️ SugarWish uses **Continental (real-time) accounting, NOT Anglo-Saxon** — 0 anglo_saxon_lines out of 24.5M `account_move_line`s (frozen Odoo); COGS is recognized at receipt/production, not on customer invoice. All product categories are **FIFO + real_time** valuation (in Odoo, `cost_method` is an `ir_property`, NOT a column on `product_category`; keyed `res_id='product.category,<id>'`). Stock journal is **STJ (`journal_id=6`, ~12.2–13.2M entries)**; vendor bills are **BILL (`journal_id=2`, ~1.6K)**. **Payment tracking happens in QuickBooks, NOT the ERP** — 99.8% of Odoo vendor bills show `payment_state='not_paid'` despite being paid; the field is never reconciled. Vendor billing is **100% manual** — accountants (Nora Stein, James Emeric, Mike, Neal) create bills AFTER goods arrive and a vendor invoice is received.

SERP COGS gaps ⚠️ **(re-verified 2026-09-11 — two of these were stale and are struck)**: ~~use Float not Decimal (precision loss)~~ — SERP's `fields.Float` is **Decimal-backed**, mapping to `DECIMAL(16,4)` (`serp_orm/fields.py:326,344`); ~~`action_reverse` references a non-existent `component_id`~~ — fixed 2026-03-05, it reads `line.product_id` (`account_move_actions.py:442`). **Still true:** the SERP ORM models declare no `stock_move_id`/`is_anglo_saxon_line`/`categ_id` fields (the Anglo-Saxon branches are deliberately omitted, matching SugarWish's Continental accounting) — note the seeded `serp_app` tables DO carry `serp_account_move.stock_move_id` and `serp_account_move_line.is_anglo_saxon_line` columns from Odoo, so don't read "no field in the ORM" as "no column"; and `amount_residual` is a plain field, not computed — the reconciliation engine that shipped 2026-07-24 deliberately does not update it. One PO ↔ many bills and one bill ↔ many POs via the M2M `serp_account_move_purchase_order_rel` (~1.9K rows in `serp_app`). The April-2026 BOM/kit expansion moved component explosion into Laravel/SERP, so Odoo SOs received **pre-expanded** components — buyer-product/packaging data **disappeared from Odoo sale orders**, breaking some finance reports (Ric flagged 2026-04-16). The `-E` SKU $0 valuation claim (Odoo-era) was not re-checked against the `serp_app` SVL — verify before quoting.

### WishDesk / SWAC / CRM

#### Three "Wish" systems are NOT the same

⚠️ **WishDesk** = the CS/CRM product (= the `jasonbkiefer/SWAC` repo, "SugarWish Activity Coordinator" — take the literal name with a grain of salt). **WishWorks** = in-house WW-#### ticket tracker via the WishBot AI Slack bot (replaced glitch reports March 2026; `/ww` self-updates from Jason's private SWIRL repo + an n8n archival step; the ticket "track" field determines repo/team ownership). **SWIRL/swirl** is BOTH (1) the company-wide AI knowledge platform (docs + MCP + Slack bot) AND (2) the WishWorks datastore (auto-generated ticket commits) — and is **separate from Jack's sw-cortex**. **SWIM** is WishDesk's AI chatbot (kb-v2 powered).

#### Two Madisons

⚠️ Don't merge them: **Madison Parks** is a SWAC dev; **Madison Meilinger** is CS/ops management (handles substitutions, placeholder/custom orders, eCard swaps).

#### Gmail tables are personal-mailbox mirrors, not ticketing

⚠️ `swcrm_z_gmail_*` mirror reps' **personal Gmail** for sales/relationship work — separate from `orders_tickets`. Thread by `gmail_thread_id` (NOT subject); CRM linkage via `linked_sw_user_id`/`linked_swcrm_lead_id`. Gmail **labels are per-mailbox real Gmail labels** (vary by user, include Google SYSTEM labels) — NOT CRM categories. AI-draft `status` must be `SENT_AS_IS` (or edited-then-sent) to count as sent; `GENERATED`/`DISCARDED` were never sent. Draft greetings leak `{{firstname}}`/`{{agent_name}}` placeholders literally (fix exists on `fix/draft-placeholder-replacement` but isn't deployed — agents hand-replace; `{{Mustache}}` and `{single}` formats both need handling). `sync_status='ERROR'` is the latest run's state, **resets on next success** — not a permanent failure.

#### `swcrm_links` is a bidirectional polymorphic graph

⚠️ `swcrm_links` has **no per-pair FK tables**; rows are stored in **BOTH directions** (A→B and B→A — dedupe by direction). `role` gives semantics ('related' ~80% = generic activity history; also `assigned_to`, `owned_by`/`owner`, `assigned_by`, `follow_up`; NULL = legacy Insightly). To find everything attached to a company/opportunity, query on BOTH `(object_name,object_id)` AND `(link_object_name,link_object_id)`. CRM "user" object = SugarWish CUSTOMER (resolve via `user_cache.user_id`, NOT `wishdesk.users.id`).

#### Opportunities: use `opportunity_state`, not legacy `state`/`status`

⚠️ `swcrm_opportunities.opportunity_state` (OPEN|WON|LOST|INVALID|UNTAPPED) is AUTHORITATIVE. The legacy `state` enum and free-text `status` are NOT for reporting. `category` (SMALL|MEDIUM|LARGE|MEGA|NA) is a **deal-size bucket, NOT a product category**. Only one 'Default' pipeline is used (3 stages: 1 Expressed Interest, 2 Active Discussion, 3 Order Paid=WON).

#### WishDesk uses Mountain Time, cookie sessions, and points at LIVE data on staging

⚠️ WishDesk timestamps are stored in **Mountain Time, NOT UTC** (a `setUTCHours(0,0,0,0)` "advance to next day" set UTC midnight = 5/6pm MT, mis-marking overnight SLAs as 4h breached — fix uses `advanceToNextDayMidnightInTimezone()` for America/Denver). The React UI uses **cookie-based sessions, NOT JWT Bearer** (Bearer only for curl/API; local dev needs `ENABLE_LOCAL_AUTH_BYPASS=true`, `APP_ENV=local`). SLA settings are NOT per-priority (all priorities get the same 60min FRT / 120min resolution targets). Genie/router config and design images live in the **sw-design repo** — editing them directly in WishDesk gets **wiped by a full-overwrite sync**. Phone/SMS comms are logged via **RingCentral** (`swcrm_ringcentral_*`), not just email.

#### `ec_order.size` → `buyer_products.id` (again, from the WishDesk side)
