⚠️ For product-size display, parse the size from `giftcards_card.product_sku` (contains "large"/"medium"/etc.) — the `ec_order.size → size_names` join is unreliable (high NULL rate; many size values like 6961 don't map). `ec_order.ship_date` is a MySQL **DATE** but returns as a UTC timestamp via mysql2 (`...T05:00:00.000Z`) — converting to MT shows the wrong day; use a date-only formatter.

#### WishDesk email threading must verify `requester_email` (security)

⚠️ `getChatSessionByThreadId()` matches by `thread_id` ALONE — bulk emails (surveys, notifications) share one Message-ID, so different customers replying get **merged into the first customer's ticket** (cross-customer contamination, 7+ prod cases). Fix: verify `requester_email == senderEmail` before merging threads.

#### Inbound email-parser is a ReDoS / app-hang vector — size-cap + bounded regex + always return 200

⚠️ A single oversized/malformed inbound email can take **all of desk.sugarwish.com down**. On 2026-06-29 a 624 KB Yahoo HTML email hit `server/services/email-parser.ts` **HTML Thread Extraction** (~line 483), whose quote-stripping `On … wrote:` regexes catastrophically backtrack (O(n²)) → 100% CPU → app hang → ALB unhealthy → site down. Two SendGrid **Inbound Parse** routes feed it: **`POST /api/email-webhook`** (`server/livechat/email-webhook-route.ts`, livechat replies, ~150 chat sessions/day) and **`POST /api/orders-email-webhook`** / `/api/orders_email_webhook` (`server/orders-tickets/email-webhook.ts`, ticket/order replies, ~3/day) — **blocking the pathway drops ALL inbound support email on that channel**, not one sender. Those two routes are configured as **FOUR Inbound Parse entries** in SendGrid (desk2, help.sugarwish.com, desk → `/api/email-webhook`, plus one whose _receiving_ host is `orders.sugarwish.com` but which POSTs cross-host to `desk.sugarwish.com/api/orders-email-webhook` — that cross-host mismatch is **intentional, do not "fix" it**). **Never delete-and-recreate a Parse entry to change it** — edit the Destination URL in place; the gap drops inbound mail with **no retry**. Env changes: desk2/desk3 are `.env` + `pm2 restart --update-env`, but **live/prod needs the var in AWS Secrets Manager followed by a Jenkins `sw-deploy-to-wishdesk-live` rebuild — a plain restart will NOT pick it up.** (WW-2103, 2026-07-28/29: added `?token=` query auth on the Parse URLs read via `SENDGRID_INBOUND_SECRET`, monitored with the `[INBOUND-AUTH] … configured=true valid=true` log line before flipping `INBOUND_WEBHOOK_AUTH_ENFORCE=true` — WW-2212 later **removed that flag** so both inbound guards always fail closed, with no monitor mode; **SendGrid Inbound Parse and the Signed Event Webhook are different features** — `SENDGRID_WEBHOOK_SECRET` governs the latter and was deferred.) It re-hung within ~1 min of every restart because the app never returned 200, so **SendGrid kept redelivering** (access log: repeated 499s). Hardening (hotfix on `live` 2026-06-29, commits `14ba3f868a` + `ad5fbcd330` — SWAC has no `main` branch): **(1)** cap HTML body size before parsing, **(2)** bound every quote-stripping regex with `{0,500}`-style limits / timeout guard instead of greedy `.*?`, **(3)** ALWAYS return HTTP 200 from the webhook so SendGrid stops the redelivery retry storm even when parsing fails. Mitigation during the incident was an out-of-band pathway block (no code change), since superseded by the merged fix.

#### WishDesk live-chat "Connection Failed" on desk2 is a socket.io transport-order bug, NOT infra

⚠️ The chat widget (`client/src/hooks/use-chat.ts`) and agent dashboard (`client/src/hooks/useAgentSocket.ts`) configure `transports: ['websocket', 'polling']` — **websocket first**. On the dev/staging tiers, nginx guards `/chat/socket.io` with HTTP Basic Auth (`.htpasswd`; **desk2 = `devuser`/`TimeToTest`**), and a browser **cannot attach cached basic-auth credentials to a native WebSocket upgrade** → nginx 401s the `wss://…/chat/socket.io/?…transport=websocket` handshake. With engine.io-client's `tryAllTransports=false` default, a failed first transport just **retries the same one forever and never falls back to polling** → widget stuck "Connection Failed." XHR/polling _does_ carry the cached basic-auth (why the rest of the site works). **Fix = flip to `transports: ['polling', 'websocket']`** (2 lines, client-only, no infra/Munyr — **not yet applied**: both hooks still say `['websocket', 'polling']` on `development` and `live`): polling connects and carries auth, then upgrades to websocket where the WS handshake is unguarded. **Prod (`desk`) is unaffected** — it has no basic auth, so it still ends up on a real WebSocket. Nuance: the raw browser WS upgrade actually fails on **both** desk2 AND desk3 (basic-auth on both) — desk3/live only work because their **AWS-ALB/HTTP-2** front lets socket.io settle onto working polling, whereas desk2's **HTTP/1.1 nginx (Hetzner)** leaves it stuck on websocket. (Evidence: end-to-end polling session driven through desk2's nginx delivered the engine.io 25s ping in real time.)

#### Socket.IO `join_room` was unauthenticated — the `agents` room leaked every live customer chat

⚠️ Until 2026-07-29 (WW-2143) **any anonymous WebSocket** could connect to the `/chat` Socket.IO namespace and emit `join_room { room: 'agents' }` with **no auth whatsoever**, joining the privileged agent broadcast room and streaming every live customer-support conversation — full message content and attachment URLs. Fix (`server/livechat/socket/connection-handler.ts`): agent identity comes **only** from a verified JWT **or a verified cookie session** — client-supplied `data.userId` is ignored entirely — on **both** the `agent-` notification branch and the main `JOIN_SESSION` branch, with a **DB role check** (authorize off the DB role, **not** a JWT claim). The client (`client/src/hooks/useAgentSocket.ts`) must send `externalJwtToken`/`jwtToken` in the `join_room` payload. **Debugging signature:** a client that omits the token is **silently rejected** — it looks like "agents stopped getting live notifications on `/admin/message-center`" with no visible error and no obvious log; check the join payload before chasing infra or transports. **History — the hole reopened for ~2 days:** the fix shipped as PR #3295 (merged 2026-08-01), but it wedged desk2's boot (see the Redis boot-hang gotcha below) so it was **reverted in PR #3300** (merged 2026-08-01, ~1h later) — reopening the hole, which SWQA re-rated **High** (unauthenticated customer-PII breach; `userId` is an enumerable auto-increment int, and id `1` exists). The re-land **PR #3305 merged 2026-08-03** (base `development`) and is now on `development`, `staging` and `live` — the hole is closed. Regression coverage on the re-land: `join-room-auth.test.ts` (24 tests) + `session-identity.test.ts` (14). **Test-vacuity footgun found there:** `SECURITY: rejects a forged agent- connection supplying only a userId` **passed against a deliberately vulnerable build** — it emitted `userId: 7` with `storage.getUser` unstubbed, so the attacker was rejected at the _DB role check_ rather than the _credential check_, guarding nothing. Any auth test here must stub `getUser` to resolve a **real** agent row so only the credential check can reject; always verify a security guard **fails with the bug reintroduced**.

#### An unbounded `await` on WishDesk's boot path wedges the process BEFORE `listen()` — pm2 says "online", nginx says 502

⚠️ **Signature:** normal module-init logs (MySQL pools + `SELECT 1`, Slack client, Qdrant OK) then **total silence** — `listen()` never runs, port 5001 never binds, `ss -ltnp` shows no listener, yet **pm2 reports both instances `online`** at 0% CPU with normal memory, and nginx logs `connect() failed (111: Connection refused) -> 127.0.0.1:5001` for every request. Deterministic across boots. This is **not** the DB and **not** the network — it is an `await` during module init that never settles. **Root cause (PR #3295, desk2 outage 2026-07-31):** `server/livechat/socket/agent-announce-gate.ts` did a bare `await redis.connect()` on a `createClient(config)` with **no `socket.connectTimeout` and no `reconnectStrategy`**. node-redis's _default_ reconnect strategy retries **indefinitely**, so against an unreachable Redis that promise **neither resolves nor rejects** — a surrounding `try/catch` is **dead code** for this failure mode, since a catch can only observe a rejection, never a pending promise. Boot chain: `server/index.ts` (`await registerRoutes`) → `server/routes.ts` (`await setupLiveChat`) → `server/livechat/index.ts` (`tryAttachAdapter` → `initAgentAnnounceGate`) → the bare `connect()`. **Diagnostic tell:** _no pending Redis socket at all_ is the clue that distinguishes the two clients — the **adapter's** clients (`socket-redis-adapter.ts` `buildClient()`) were already bounded and failed fast/closed; the **gate's** second client is the one that hung. **Rules:** (1) every client created during module init needs a **bounded** connect — `connectTimeout` + a fail-fast `reconnectStrategy` (return an `Error` before the first successful connect) **plus** a `withTimeout()` race (`server/livechat/with-timeout.ts`) so "boot cannot hang" is a property of _our_ call, not of the library's internals; (2) boot must **fail OPEN** — a Redis outage degrades live chat to the in-memory gate, it must never stop the process binding its port; (3) **never latch an `initialized` flag before the attempt** — doing so made one boot-time blip pin the whole fleet to in-memory gates permanently, silently defeating the documented 30s retry; (4) bound the hot-path (`claimAgentAnnouncement`, 2s) and shutdown (`quit()` with a `disconnect()` fallback, else SIGTERM hangs and pm2 SIGKILLs). **Same class, different route (pre-existing, STILL OPEN — fix PR #3306 was CLOSED unmerged; `origin/live` still has the bare `app.listen` callback):** `server/swim/mcp-server/index.ts` wraps `app.listen()` in `new Promise(resolve => …)` with **no reject path and no `'error'` listener** — a bind failure (`EADDRINUSE`/`EACCES`) is reported **asynchronously as an `'error'` event**, `listen()` does not throw, so the promise never settles; the retry loop only catches rejections (dead code) and the manager's own `uncaughtException` handler **logs without exiting**, swallowing the crash. Only reachable with `ENABLE_MCP_SERVER=true`. Fix: `new Promise((resolve, reject) => { const s = app.listen(port, host, resolve); s.once('error', reject); })` + a `withTimeout()` on the awaited call. **Repro without desk2:** run the build locally with an unreachable/absent `REDIS_URL` (`REDIS_URL=redis://127.0.0.1:1`), or on the box `PORT=5099 timeout 90 node dist/index.js` and read the last log line before the hang. Related: `server/redis-session-store.ts` logs the **raw `REDIS_URL` twice on boot** into pm2 logs — managed ElastiCache URLs carry inline auth (`rediss://:TOKEN@host`). The scrub was part of the closed #3306, so it is **still unfixed** on `origin/live` and `development` (~L150/L154); `describeRedisTarget()` (host:port only) exists only in `server/livechat/socket-redis-adapter.ts`.

---

### Proposals, Redemption & Migration

#### `proposals.details_json` is a multi-writer bug factory

⚠️ `proposals.details_json` is written by MANY paths (quiz, genie, agent, admin, merchandise, duplicate, repeating-order, MCP tool), each populating a different subset of ~25 keys with different defaults (root cause of WW-460/WW-650). **8 universally-required fields:** `sender_id, product_id, product_sku, product_configuration_id, quantity, delivery_method, price, product_selections (non-empty)`. **Validate at the CART-ADD boundary, NOT creation; never blanket-validate; any silent fallback MUST throw/alert** (silent `console.log` = the bug). The merchandise preview-flow bug let merch be purchased with empty `designs[]`/`print_image_url=NULL` → SWOP renders nothing silently (Jason hard-blocked add-to-cart with `designs.length===0` → HTTP 400 on 2026-05-15).

#### Redemption resolves products at REDEMPTION time — consolidation has a long tail

⚠️ **AI assumes** consolidation is safe because orders freeze the product at creation.
✅ **Reality:** Redemption/receiver resolves products at **REDEMPTION time**, so it hits **disabled child SKUs** after a consolidation. The April-2026 combo-line migration (Phase 2 archived child variant SKUs: `archive=1`, `status='disabled'`, `inventory_link=NULL`) caused: orders stuck at the Odoo-sync errored state (`oddo_synchronized=3`, "product not found"); ~14K giftcard `card_id`s pointing at the CORE product (type 51) instead of sweetshoppe (Jaypee corrected); ~14K active ecards with old standalone selections showing empty "no items" redemption screens. **Lesson: consolidation needs historical-card backfill.** The WW-510 disabled-SKU bypass: the resume flow skips the choose-page localStorage filter, and the migration NULLed `inventory_link` removing the `linkedProduct()` safety net — add a pre-submission guard (`archive=0, status='enabled', deleted_at IS NULL`).

---

### Team & Process

#### Devs are NOT interchangeable; glitch ≠ bug

⚠️ Offshore devs aren't interchangeable: **Manish** = lead / most Odoo-experienced / SERP secondary (owns migration scripts, and now the post-cutover cleanup of `laravel_live` `serp_*` tables and Odoo sidecars); **Subash** = Laravel-track; **Parish** = WishDesk/SWAC-track lead merger; **Aashish** = junior, low-risk only. Validation/product changes need sign-off from **Caley/Clare/Kelley**; **Anna** triages; **Seth** owns external API accounts/infra; **Munyr** owns Jenkins CI/CD + the AWS→Hetzner migration (Jack is a consumer, not infra owner). **Devs only REPLICATE glitches, never fix them directly** — a separate BUG ticket must be Product-prioritized; fixing a glitch directly violates process. Irreproducible bugs are NOT chased — "fixed" often means a one-off DATA PATCH for one customer while the systemic fix lags (e.g. the ecard-empty-redemption bug was patched customer-by-customer for weeks before Jaypee bulk-fixed ~14K).

---

### Concurrency, Deadlocks & Multiple Writers

⚠️ **Multiple systems write the same MySQL rows.** Laravel, Retool, and n8n all write `receiver_products` (and others), causing `ER_LOCK_DEADLOCK` and partial updates; Retool can overwrite the auto-managed `updated_at`. The Odoo-era n8n "Disable_Unreserved_Products" / "Auto-Disable Workflow" (`UPDATE receiver_products WHERE inventory_link=parent`, ~1 min cadence) was a known deadlock source competing with Laravel/Retool writers — and it **re-disabled a product within seconds of a manual re-enable** if still below drop_level (no cooldown; disable-only, never re-enables). n8n state today: "Disable Unreserved Products V2" (`VRdmXlm2XTeRbOyT`) is **inactive ("Disabled During SERP Cutover")**; the active successor **"Disable Unreserved Products - Cutover"** (`CGHWrz8FzJYFHQH0`) runs **every 30 min** against `laravel_live` view `receiver_product_inventory` (`sellable_qty + reservation_hold_qty < drop_level` with `unreserved_outbound_qty > 0`) and is currently **NOTIFY-ONLY** — its `0. Flag` node `disable_actions_enabled=false` posts to `#jack-test` and disables nothing; flipping it to `true` makes it disable parent + child SKUs and post to `#ops-and-tech`. The Laravel inventory deadlock crisis (escalating to every ~2 min on >50-row batches, Jan 2025) comes from three check-then-act races: `ReceiverProduct::decrementInventory()` (SELECT-then-UPDATE without `lockForUpdate()`), `PrepickOrderJob` batch (decrements before order confirmation, no batch transaction), and `InventoryReservationService::canReReserveProducts()` (read-check without `FOR UPDATE`). SERP uses atomic `INSERT...ON DUPLICATE KEY UPDATE`; Laravel uses non-atomic SELECT-then-UPDATE — with **no cross-system coordination**, both can independently oversell the same inventory. `serp_stock_move` has **no row-level locking in workers**, enabling duplicates if run in parallel.

⚠️ **One open transaction in `serp_app` stalls every order-queue shipment.** Posting a journal entry takes a `FOR UPDATE` lock on the latest move of that journal (`_generate_move_name`, `serp_orm/models/account_move_actions.py` ~L1061) and holds it for the whole enclosing transaction. The SERP **dev console** (`routers/admin/dev_console_ws.py`) keeps one open transaction per session until an explicit commit/rollback, the 30-min idle timeout, or pod death — so a dry-run that posts STJ entries (inventory apply, `action_revalue`) and is left uncommitted makes every shipment's STJ post fail with MySQL **1205** (lock wait timeout). That is what the 2026-09-28 ~14:09–14:29 UTC burst of `Worker failure: serp-order-queue (RuntimeError)` in #serp-alerts was (13 of 29 shipments failed; a "resume" that opened a second session left the orphan holding the lock until the pod was deleted). When shipments 1205 on the STJ, look for an uncommitted dev-console session first; commit or roll back promptly after any dev-console run that posts JEs. Long MO-unlink scans (see the FK-index entry under Performance Footguns) cause the same stall.

---

### Analytics, Reporting & Alert Hygiene

⚠️ **"Sales with sleeves"** is the **% of total ecard revenue that includes a sleeve**, NOT the dollar value of sleeves. `receiver_product_status.*_slack_ts` columns are **alert-dedup guards (already-sent markers), NOT message bodies**. Most `:rotating_light:`/`#api-autofix` posts are routine **auto-resolved noise** ("No action on data/APIs should be necessary from alerts in #api-autofix" — Seth); a single alert is usually not actionable unless it explicitly says the auto-fix failed. n8n workflow exports are **snapshots** — the `active` flag is unreliable and workflow IDs differ from live; check `n8n.sugarwish.com` for actual state. Since the cutover the Odoo-dependent n8n workflows (Odoo Sync Error Handler, Order Sync Integrity Monitor, Sync Receiver Product Inventory with Odoo, Core SKU Live Warnings (sellable_qty), Odoo inventory in wrong location) are **inactive ("Disabled During SERP Cutover")** with `- Cutover` successors active (`Core SKU Live Warnings - Cutover`, `Inventory In Wrong Location - Cutover`). The old Odoo→Google-Sheets export (`sw_excel_update`, hourly crons to sheet `1ESYinwhFztJfzo0Aec3BwafS9d7cHxWTUhh9vpVGGJA`) **logged errors but didn't raise** — failures were silent; the n8n "Sheets Export - serp_app" workflow is the active successor.

---

### Pre-cutover gotchas (history)

Everything here concerns the darklaunch / dual-write era and the Laravel↔Odoo integration. **Darklaunch (dual-write validation, <1% drift gate) is RETIRED** — switched off at the 2026-09-26 cutover; the drift-monitor package is deleted and the `darklaunch-drift`, `publish-serp-kits`, `reconcile-published-stock` crons are `active=0`/RETIRED in `crons.py`; `SERP_DARKLAUNCH_ENABLED`, `SERP_SHADOW_WRITES_ENABLED`, `USE_SERP_AS_LIVE` and `ENVIRONMENT_SET` have zero hits in SERP `backend/`. Keep this for reading frozen Odoo data, old commits, and pre-9/26 rows.

#### The Laravel↔Odoo integration was PULL-based, not push

❌ **AI assumed** Laravel pushes orders to Odoo.
✅ **Reality (pre-cutover):** **Odoo was the puller.** Odoo crons authenticated to Laravel's `/api/odoo/*` endpoints (Bearer token), pulled unsynced orders, then called back to mark passed/failed. Laravel had **no XML-RPC client**. Cron `Sugarwish: Update Failed Orders` re-pulled every 10 min; `Update Orders` every 6 min; `Update Prepicks` every 120 min; `Update Failed Prepicks` every 15 min (27 active Odoo crons in total — all **frozen** now). SERP's dual-write XML-RPC capability was new and had to be built. **Replaced by** the `serp_order_queue_worker` ingest above.

#### Laravel ↔ Odoo sync bugs (archived / not-yet-created SKUs)

⚠️ Archiving/disabling a SKU on one side without the other broke SERPY sync. Inventory added to Odoo **before** the SKU existed in Laravel never got picked up. The old diagnostic "orders stuck at `oddo_synchronized=3` reference archived/disabled SKUs" **could not be used as written — 0 rows with value 3 in prod** (that column is now `oddo_synchronized(deleted)`). The underlying failure (a SKU archived on one side) was real; find it by the SKU mismatch, not by `=3`. Common root cause: matching Odoo `product_product.active=false` (archived variant) while the template stays active.

#### Odoo BOMs/quants and XML-RPC limits

⚠️ "We don't use Odoo phantom BOMs anymore." Phantom BOMs **cannot** be created via the Odoo API (UI only). Inventory corrections = inventory adjustments, not phantom-BOM consumption. SERP's own phantom-BOM concept is separate. **Odoo.sh 330s timeout:** prod Odoo had a 330-second statement timeout; `NOT IN`/`!=`/`<>` on indexed columns of multi-million-row tables → seq scan → exceeds 330s (positive `IN`-lists fix it; see Performance Footguns). **Odoo XML-RPC** rejected any method starting with `_` ("Private methods cannot be called remotely") — hit when calling `stock.move._action_confirm`/`_action_done`/`_action_assign`; use public equivalents (`stock.picking.button_validate`, `purchase.order.button_confirm`) or create child records directly. Odoo also rejected quant creation on consumable/service products ("Quants cannot be created for consumables or services"), so Serpy inventory adjustments on consu/service failed at sync. **Do NOT call `button_mark_done` via XML-RPC** for MOs — it cancels component moves and creates a zero-qty backorder; use the `mrp.immediate.production` wizard's `process()`. Inventory adjustments used a direct `stock.move` in `done` state via virtual location id **14**. (SERP's Odoo XML-RPC handler arms were deleted 2026-09-26, commit `90e712f39`; only the read-only `odoo_xmlrpc_service.py` remains.)

#### Timezone (~6h offset is BY DESIGN) — still matters when comparing to Odoo

⚠️ SERP stores **naive Mountain Time** (America/Denver); Odoo PG stores **naive UTC**. Every comparison against frozen Odoo data MUST convert Denver wall-clock to UTC accounting for DST (−7h MST / −6h MDT). Seeding incident: a laptop in Eastern stamped `darklaunch_cutover_at` 2h off, creating a dead zone — always use the time of the Odoo prod seed, NOT laptop-local `NOW()`. `darklaunch_cutover_at` was per-env in `serp_darklaunch_meta` (prod `2026-06-04 09:27:20`, staging `2026-06-03 11:52:27`; primary cutover ts `2026-05-30 14:14`); `serp_darklaunch_meta` was **DROPPED 2026-08-03** (commit `809a4d8f9`). Display side: a frontend `new Date("YYYY-MM-DD")` parses as UTC midnight and renders one day early in Denver, and SERP routers disagree on whether they emit naive UTC or naive Denver — before touching any date display or date truncation, check which zone the source stores and which one the formatter assumes; server-side use `fields.Date.today()` (Denver-aware), not `date.today()`.

#### Dual-write failure modes

⚠️ `stock_picking` stuck in `confirmed`/`assigned` (expected `done`) usually = (1) `sugarwish_integration` overrides `button_validate` calling private `_action_done` (blocked over XML-RPC); (2) `put_product_qty` wizard → empty API → `JSONDecodeError`; (3) **UoM mismatch** (sync sends `uom_id=1` Units but product is `lb`) — look up the product's real `uom_id` first. SERP = atomic upsert; Laravel = SELECT-then-UPDATE → cross-system oversell risk.

#### Picking creation: `action_confirm` does NOT create a picking (permanent divergence)

⚠️ `sale.order.action_confirm` deliberately does NOT trigger procurement or create a delivery picking. The order worker is the **SINGLE** picking creator (`stock.picking.action_process_new_order`) — adding a second creator on `action_confirm` produces two pickings per order. Intentionally omitted: no `procurement.group`, no lot/serial, no package/owner tracking, chatter only on document-level models (`ir.model.data`/`env.ref()` ARE implemented, `serp_orm/models/ir_model_data.py`). (The worker was the "darklaunch worker" pre-cutover; today it is `serp_order_queue_worker`.)

#### Odoo "approved differences" — intentional, not bugs (still useful as Odoo-parity notes)

⚠️ SERP's intentional divergences are listed in `docs/ODOO_APPROVED_DIFFERENCES.md` (read the current file — the list shrinks as parity work lands). Current: `sale.order.action_confirm` creates no procurement/picking (§2.1); `mail.template` by `template_ref` (§2.2); `stock.move.reservation_date` not computed (§2.3); receipts without a picking (§2.4); partial `remove_component` (§2.10); `_action_confirm` bypass to `assigned` (§2.11); removal strategy always `fifo` (§2.12); mostly-Continental accounting, no reconciliation engine, `account.move.name` without `ir.sequence` (§3); no `procurement.group`, chatter only on document-level models, M2M cascade in Python (§4); editable lines on confirmed POs and `product_qty <= 0` rejected (§5.1–5.2). **PO "Send by Email" sends no email (§5.3) is an OPEN GAP, not an approved difference** — it flips draft→sent and tells the user to email the vendor manually. **No longer divergences:** sale `invoice_status` and PO `effective_date`/`date_planned` now compute like Odoo 15 (§2.8 dropped 2026-09-28) — old notes saying "the worker creates SOs at `invoice_status='to invoice'` and never advances" are stale. Historical items from older notes: `res.partner._increase_rank` supplier_rank bumps on PO confirm; PO-receipt/vendor-bill recompute fields; Odoo crons (reservation/ship) that move state post-create — **but NOT prepick, which IS replicated** (`_action_assign` pre-fills `qty_done`; see `ODOO_APPROVED_DIFFERENCES.md` §2.7, which reverses the older framing); `procurement.group` model; lot/serial tracking; package/owner tracking; line-level chatter; `button_confirm` allows empty PO (test-pinned); by-product cost carve-out (dead path — no SugarWish BOM defines a by-product, `mrp_bom_byproduct` count 0; there is no `serp_mrp_bom_byproduct` table in `serp_app`); sale-order names `f"S{ec_order_id}"`. ~~No bin-level putaway~~ was **retracted 2026-09-11** (see the `_gather()` entry — SERP DOES resolve to child bins; the drift-monitor suppression of that item may have hidden a real corruption signal).

#### Most "drift" was NOT a bug (Drift Monitor, retired)

⚠️ The Drift Monitor diffed **Odoo prod (the PG source of truth)** column-by-column against `serp_*` shadows, joining on `odoo_id`. Most flagged "drift" was expected:

- **Post-seed/post-reseed Odoo staleness** (Odoo crons/users edited rows AFTER the seed snapshot; cleared by reseed — drift count often equalled post-cutover write count; e.g. all 79 drifting `stock_quant` rows had post-reseed Odoo write dates, `real_bug` = none).
- **By-design worker-row divergence** on create-time columns (suppressed in `WORKER_ROW_DIVERGENT_COLUMNS`): `sale_order.name`='S'+ec_order_id vs Odoo `ir.sequence`; `sale_order_line.sequence`=10 always vs Odoo's 10/300/301; worker skips Odoo's NULL-product separator lines; `write_uid`/`create_uid`; picking name drift; float precision noise.
- **~6h DATETIME drift** from Denver↔UTC conversion (seeder stored Odoo naive-UTC as MT wall-clock).
- **Reservation timing lag** and **stock_quant freshness gap** (Odoo reserved continuously faster than the worker polled — one-directional `darklaunch_quant = odoo_quant − N`; converged next poll).
- **2026-07-02 drift sweep (PR #361):** Preselect GMV bug — `_recompute_sale_order_totals` excluded packaging/component lines from EVERY preselect (PR #275 over-generalized from an unredeemed exemplar); redeemed preselects now sum ALL lines, cold-cancels keep the bare-gift total. Also: orphan seeded quants now deleted by `delete_orphan_quants`; both-side-cancelled pickings excluded from picking-state counts.
- Benign value drifts: `invoice_status` (odoo=`invoiced` vs dl=`to invoice`), date/tz ISO-Z vs local, NULL commitment_date/effective_date.

The only signal that mattered was the per-table `values:` line: "K rows, C columns — clean" = faithful; "X/Y rows diverge" = real drift; ":red_circle: N rows 30m+ old in Odoo MISSING from darklaunch" = genuine sync gap. Real bugs were a minority: dropped order lines when `product.default_code` is NULL, seeder collapsing `partially_available`→`assigned`, bin-level `location_id` divergence (cause **unknown** — the old "SERP doesn't replicate Odoo putaway/removal strategy" explanation was retracted 2026-09-11).

**Drift workflow was code-generated.** The n8n "Darklaunch Drift Monitor" JSON was generated by `scripts/build_darklaunch_drift_n8n.py` (**deleted 2026-06-11**, workflow inactive; the n8n "Darklaunch Compare" workflow is also inactive) and the monitor then moved to an in-repo cron (`workers/darklaunch_drift/worker.py`), itself `active=0` and now deleted. Design history: loop-free (no SplitInBatches; it stalled ~14/42 tables and never fired Slack), a **Merge barrier** (Wait For Both — without it, fires on the first branch only → false flags), `_equiv` normalization (date-only vs datetime tolerance, NULL↔'0.0000', float ±0.01, bool↔0/1), hourly cron `0 6-18 * * 1-5` (6am–6pm MT Mon–Fri), latest ~1000 ids per table across ~42 tables, alerts to `#jack-test` (`C083M27KU8L`) for rows 30m+ old that were missing or settled-drift (`GRACE_MINUTES=30`, `WINDOW=1000`). Slack `messageType:block` blocksUi-only throws `no_text` — use `messageType:text` with `mrkdwn`.

**4-layer noise suppression (PR #457, migration 056, deployed 2026-07-09):** L1 boundary-safe windowing (GL/account_move jitter → ~0); L2 age/recency grace (today + 6h); L3 a persistence gate + delta-trend backed by `serp_app.drift_sweep_history` (a finding must persist and NOT be shrinking before it alerts); L4 a benign-classifier collapsing known-benign items into an `ℹ️ known-benign` footer. Safety nets: a **material-shrink floor (≥10%/sweep)** so a slow-trickling real drift (Δ360→359→358) never reads "converging" forever, plus **per-section hard ceilings**. The first ~2 post-deploy sweeps looked un-suppressed (L3 needs history). Layered on the standing "neither LAG nor DRIFT is ever an acceptable answer" rule.

#### Darklaunch isolation rule (history) — NEVER read Odoo values at runtime to "fix" drift

⚠️ **AI tried** to fix a worker discrepancy by reading the "correct" value from live Odoo at runtime. **Forbidden.** The worker queried Odoo **only to resolve IDs to stamp into `odoo_id`** — never Odoo VALUES. Everything (products by SKU/`default_code`, locations) resolved against the **local seeded DB**, so many drift/cost bugs traced to the **SEED, not runtime**. If local `serp_product_product.default_code` is NULL, the worker **drops that order line** (fix: backfill `default_code` / seed `sugarwish_id→product` map / fix worker resolution — **not** suppress drift or add Odoo reads).

#### `odoo_id` stamping was fragile (`odoo_id_stamper`, retired 2026-06-25)

⚠️ `odoo_id_stamper` (`workers/odoo_id_stamper.py`, file no longer present on `origin/main`) was a separate post-create step on its OWN darklaunch pool connection and the **only** place the worker touched Odoo PG. Failure modes:

- Ran while the worker's txn was still uncommitted under REPEATABLE READ → its snapshot couldn't see the worker's own picking → fell back to natural-key matching that **mis-stamped a committed neighbor picking** (adjacent qty=1 orders share product_id → non-unique keys; observed batch ~84% NULL, ~16% mis-stamped).
- Must stamp **VARIANT** id on `serp_product_product` and **TEMPLATE** id on `serp_product_template` (was a bug).
- A dead idle Odoo PG connection (SSL closed) silently broke it → rows landed `odoo_id=NULL` → the Drift Monitor reported them "missing". **Signature:** `MAX(odoo_id)` frontier FROZEN while `MAX(id)` climbs; latest rows ~100% `odoo_id=NULL`. Fixed with a liveness-pinging pool (`_acquire_live_sync_conn()`: `SELECT 1`, drain dead handles, capped exponential backoff 0.25s→2s, recycle at 120s, `keepalives_idle 30`, `connect_timeout 10`).
- `/compare-darklaunch` only diffed rows where `odoo_id IS NOT NULL` — unstamped children (stock_move/move_line/quant with `odoo_id=NULL`) were silently EXCLUDED (false "no overlap"). The stamper had to be wired into EVERY write-path; real prod bug fixed 2026-06-03: `stamp_child_odoo_ids` was wired only into the Serpy sync-queue path, not the order path → thousands of `odoo_id=NULL` move/line rows.

#### Four distinct shadow/replica DBs (legacy) — don't conflate them

⚠️ Legacy names still appear in code, configs and old notes. Local SERP DBs are now `serp_local_staging` / `serp_local_prod` (old env values normalise onto them).

| DB                                  | What it was                                                                                                           | Flag                         |
| ----------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------- |
| `serp_*_replica` (staging/prod)     | Clean row-for-row mirror of manage/laravel_live, **zero Odoo overlay**, nearly empty shells                           | —                            |
| `serp_*_darklaunch` (staging/prod)  | Replica **plus** Odoo overlay (normal BOMs, MOs, SVL, POs) where the worker dual-wrote                                | `SERP_DARKLAUNCH_ENABLED`    |
| `serp_shadow` (Hetzner)             | Predecessor prod-traffic handler validation                                                                           | `SERP_SHADOW_WRITES_ENABLED` |
| `serp_test` (Hetzner `5.161.233.240`) | **STAGING SERP DB** (has darklaunch history; not a throwaway; prod is `serp_app`), MCP key `serp_test`             | —                            |

- Fingerprints: darklaunch had `_migrations` (`serp_darklaunch_meta` dropped 2026-08-03 — no longer a fingerprint); replicas had neither and no Odoo-owned/manufacturing tables. "**compare-replica**" tooling actually compared **darklaunch**, not the replica. When `SERP_DARKLAUNCH_ENABLED=false`, behavior had to be identical to before ("if dark launch is false, then don't set anything").

#### The darklaunch worker was intentionally slow — do NOT "optimize" (moot post-cutover)

⚠️ **AI tried** to batch/parallelize the darklaunch worker. Slowness was **deliberate** for costing parity. ⚠️ **Timings corrected 2026-09-11: prod ~1.02s mean / 1.34s p95 per shipment** — the old "~27s local / 47–66s prod" was a local bench artifact, off by ~40x. Still serial, ~130 per-move UPDATEs, to match Odoo's `account_move.sequence_number` ordering. Batching broke `/compare-costing` parity. Throughput ceiling ~72 shipments/hr (~1,728/day) — **6–7× below** the Dec-2025 peak (peak day shipments 11,433 on 2025-12-29; peak hour 1,877 orders). The current `serp_order_queue_worker` is the successor — re-measure before quoting throughput.

⚠️ **The worker blocked the shared asyncio event loop.** All workers run in ONE process on ONE loop; `darklaunch._poll_cycle` was `async def` but did synchronous blocking PyMySQL work with no `asyncio.to_thread` and no yield — it **starved the odoo-sync worker** (live 2026-05-29: odoo-sync stalled ~95 pending, synced once per darklaunch cycle). Fix: wrap processing in `asyncio.to_thread` like `order_queue_worker` does. Separate incident: "Sync cycle wedged: 3 consecutive timeouts at 180s" is transient/self-recovering (idle-dropped socket), distinct from starvation.

⚠️ **Newest-first windowing permanently starved the backlog tail.** Detectors (`detect_shipped_orders_odoo`, `detect_cancelled_orders_odoo`) had a vestigial `ORDER BY ... DESC LIMIT 50`; with ~883 in-flight candidates (765 receiver + 118 preselect) the oldest low-id tail was **never re-checked** and stayed `confirmed` forever. Fix: **oldest-first** ordering (by replica `serp_stock_picking.create_date`, NOT live DATE-granular `ship_date`) + a per-cycle processing cap. (`serp_stock_picking.state` has no `partially_available` value — that filter element is dead.)

#### PM2 / AWS-EC2 operational gotchas (frozen legacy host)

⚠️ On the old AWS EC2 (`34.203.231.65`), inline `pm2 reload` over SSH failed — you had to export `PATH=/home/ubuntu/.nvm/versions/node/v20.20.1/bin:$PATH; PM2_HOME=/home/ubuntu/.pm2`. **Before rebuilding the darklaunch DB you MUST `pm2 stop serp-workers`** or MySQL 1412 "table definition changed" errors killed in-flight shipments (resume after). The old `deploy.sh` did NOT run schema migrations against the prod manage cluster (`npm run migrate` was local-only; `seeding/migrations/*.sql` deleted at HEAD; deploy hardcoded the docker `serp_staging_darklaunch`), so new `serp_*` tables/columns needed a **manual migration of the live manage DB BEFORE code shipped** (Manish + DBA). Also: `serp_*` tables existed only in `laravel_local`; `laravel_staging` and `live_staging` had only `serp_audit_logs` and `serp_users` (no Alembic migrations ever applied to staging). None of this applies to the K3s/`serp_app` setup (runner-applied migrations, auto-deploy).

---

## Terminology / Glossary

SugarWish's internal vocabulary, SKU encoding scheme, product taxonomy, warehouse jargon, and system/product names — the decoder ring for the codes, prefixes, and names that appear throughout the data.

**Business model:** corporate/personal gifting. Buyer sends an **eCard** → **recipient** clicks through and **chooses their own gift** → ships to their door. The recipient (not buyer) picks the SKU at redemption. Catalog: candy, snacks, cookies, popcorn, eCards, flowers, custom mugs/merch, custom-branded merchandise — not candy-only.

**Warehouse jargon:** "**shop**" (verb) = warehouse pick/pack a gift order (NOT browsing or customer-facing shopping). "**pick**" (noun) = treat count in a size (2/4/6/8/12/16-pick → box/shipper size). "**shopped and shipped**" = packed and sent.

**Gift sizes (ascending price):** Mini → Small → Medium → Large → Larger → X-Large → Grand → Deluxe (plus shipping fees).

### SKU Prefix / Suffix Taxonomy

SKU prefixes and suffixes are **NOT arbitrary or opaque** — they encode role/stage, product line, location, and ownership. Field is `default_code` (SERP `serp_product_product` / frozen Odoo `product_product`) = `sku` (Laravel).

#### Role / Stage Prefixes

| Prefix       | Meaning                                                                             | Notes                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **RM-**      | Raw Material                                                                        | Loose/bulk individual component as received; bought via POs; tracked **in SERP (`serp_app`) only** — no Laravel RM (pre-cutover it was Odoo). ~1,000–1,033 active (pre-cutover count). e.g. `RM-40-094-A`, `RM-19-044-A`                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **SA-**      | Sub-Assembly / saleable stockable finished good                                     | Carries `sugarwish_id`; has a normal BOM in `serp_app.serp_mrp_bom`; built from RMs. ~2,100 active (pre-cutover count). e.g. `SA-03-025-A`, `SA-15-014-A`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| **FG-**      | Finished-Goods kit                                                                  | Also a sellable receiver product. ~194 (pre-cutover count). e.g. `FG-10-228-A-4PACK`, `FG-48-001-A` (Sweet Dreams Large)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **B-**       | Box (inner colored retail box / gift box)                                           | Tracked by **Laravel kits**, NOT SERP phantom BOMs. Pattern `B-{TYPE}-{era}-{YY}-{SIZE}` (e.g. `B-A01-REG-24-RED-MIN`); winter pattern `B-{TYPE}-WTR-{YY}-001-{SIZE}`. `B-AAA`=Type A Candy, `B-B02`=Type B Popcorn, `B-CCC`=Type C Cookies/Coffee/Tea. **B-LLL cocktail boxes** (6 sizes) have NO BOMs at all (standalone packaging) and are excluded from forecast via product_type 17/18                    |
| **S-**       | Shipper (outer corrugated mailing box) OR sweets/food component (context-dependent) | In BOM-line context S- denotes shipper/sweets; cocktail shippers use S-LLL                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **C-**       | Carton / Cup / Card (context-dependent)                                             | Cups/cartons/cards packaging                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| **I-**       | Insert (cup/tray insert, packing tissue, foam insert, printed insert cards)         | e.g. hot-sauce foam `I-A46-REG-25-FOM-{MIN/SML/MED/LRG/XLG}`; year token matters (`I-GXX-REG-24-CAR-TON`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| **L-**       | Label / Sticker                                                                     | e.g. popcorn sticker `L-B02-REG-23-0NN-COX`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **P-**       | Production Paper / Sticker                                                          | Used in ~525–704 BOMs (≈1 per kit, virtually every order); e.g. `P-XXX-REG-22-SKR-XXX`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| **M-**       | Mug / merchandise (blank mug / custom branded mug)                                  | e.g. `M-CCC-WHT-26-MIR-S32`, `M-CCC-WHT-26`; also `M-CEW-`. Custom-branded merch is SERP-tracked                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **T-**       | Tape                                                                                | BOM lines frequently `product_qty=0` (optional) → all currently inactive                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **E-**       | Extra (care cards, coasters, extras)                                                |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| **G-**       | Gift wrap / rare (1 row)                                                            |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| **X-**       | Rare (11 rows)                                                                      |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| **V- / VB-** | Vendor / Wine packaging-components (Vinebox)                                        |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| lowercase words / `h_*` / `a_*` / `c_*` | Saleable kit/category/sleeve box-size products (often phantom-BOM)  | See Box Vocabularies                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

- `WW-###`/`SUG-###` = Jira/WishWorks ticket prefixes (unrelated to SKUs). `swcrm_`/`ec_`/`component_` = table prefixes (unrelated).
- **RM → SA** via Manufacturing Order (one RM + one carton → SA).
- **Ownership encoding:** **SA-/RM-/FG-** recipes live in **SERP BOMs** (`serp_app.serp_mrp_bom`; frozen Odoo `mrp_bom` holds pre-cutover history); **B-/S-** (box/shipper) live in **Laravel kits**. Kit BOMs (phantom) for on-order explosion live in `laravel_live.serp_mrp_bom` (intended SoT for kits — see Kit / BOM Vocabulary).
- ❌ **AI treats SKU prefixes as opaque** → ✅ **Reality:** they encode role/stage, product line, location, and which system owns the recipe.

#### SKU Numeric Segment & Tokens

- **Numeric segment 2 = product line / product_type** — extracted from SKU position 2 (e.g. `SA-`**`01`**`-025-A` → type 01 = Candy → maps to `product_type.id`). Common: `01`/`156`=candy, `02`=popcorn, `03`=cookies, `10`/`14`=wine, `19`=coffee, `20`=tea, `21`=cocoa, `40`=socks/merch, `45`=brownies. Packaging SKUs (B/C/I/L/S/M/P/E) do **NOT** get a product_type assigned.
- **Box SKU token order:** `[type][product-letter]-REG-[YEAR]-[color]-[size]`. **Year token (22 vs 25)** signals a versioned box/packaging swap (`REG-22` → `REG-25`); BOMs must be swapped when ops switches. Year-22 components are NOT obsolete — e.g. size 6916 (Grand Bakery Cafe) and 6962 (Large Custom Mug & Treats) consistently use year-22 packaging, so "year-22 EXTRA" alerts are **false positives** (the n8n workflow that raised them no longer exists in the live n8n list).
- **System token (A/B/C)** is only meaningful for packaging SKUs; SA/RM/FG always show system as null/blank.

#### Location Suffixes: -A vs -E

| Suffix | Meaning                                                                         |
| ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **-A** | Standard / **Taylor** (location_id 2); the base SKU                             |
| **-E** | Branded **Englewood** (EW, location_id 1); used when a sleeve/merch is attached |

- Orders with a sleeve/merch **must** use `-E`; non-branded **must** use `-A` (the hourly n8n workflow `Check Correct Product Used` (active) flags mismatches on Bakery & Cafe 567 and Custom Mug & Treats 47 orders; Custom Mug & Treats SKUs must contain `-E-custommug`). Taylor was retired as a ship-from location at cutover (all vinebox_states ship_from=1), so -A vs -E is now a branding distinction.
- The forecast rolls `-A` and `-E` as the **same product**.
- A Serpy request "move quantity -A to -E" = a **PAIR of inventory adjustments**, never a stock_transfer.
- When `-E` lacks a BOM, the fix is a synthetic BOM pointing at `RM-XX-XXX-E` or a duplicated `-A` BOM with A→E swap.
- **EW Bakery Cafe rename convention** (James Emeric, 2026-04-30): change the trailing `A`→`E` to mark the EW version; 68 such SKUs (cookies SA-03, coffee SA-19, tea SA-20, hot cocoa SA-21, brownies SA-45), all initially disabled.
- ❌ **AI assumes** `-A`/`-E` are arbitrary variants → ✅ **Reality:** -A=Taylor/base, -E=Englewood/with-box; match new products and historical sales to -E for branded orders.
- **-E $0 valuation (pre-cutover, frozen Odoo history):** -E SKUs from the May-2026 -A→-E migration were valued at $0 in Odoo (~$28–37k stranded value; found 2026-06-24) because the paired -E inbound `inventory-adjustment` move landed with `price_unit=NULL` and no cost basis, so FIFO stamped every inbound SVL layer at `unit_cost=0`. Editing `standard_price` never fixes it (it only seeds the _next_ inbound layer; the Stock Valuation report sums `SVL.remaining_value`). Jack fixed it 2026-06-24 with Odoo 15's Product Revaluation wizard (`stock.valuation.layer.revaluation`; counterpart = Cost of Goods Sold, confirmed by accounting; scripting it server-side must set `company_id` on the `stock_valuation_layer_revaluation` row — NOT NULL, a raw insert does not default it; helper scripts `scripts/odoo_revalue_e_skus*.py` (removed)); the historical $0-COGS shipments remain a separate closed-period P&L item. The general lesson carries to SERP: a value-only SVL revaluation, not a cost-card edit, is what moves valuation. The same pattern exists in `serp_app` SVL: about 72 open `SA-*-E` layers across 41 products with `unit_cost=0` (~4,000 units, ~$8.8k `remaining_value`) as of 2026-09-29.

### `sugarwish_id` / `odoo_id` Prefix Encoding

Cross-system identity bridges. They are **fabricated/encoded**, not raw foreign keys.

#### `product_template.sugarwish_id` — THE external sync key

Lives on **`product_template`, NOT `product_product`**. It is the authoritative bridge between the ERP product and Laravel (Odoo originally; carried into SERP via `odoo_id` seeding). Decode by prefix (the column exists only on frozen Odoo `product_template`; `serp_product_template` has no `sugarwish_id`, so in SERP the bridge is `odoo_id`/SKU):

| sugarwish_id value    | Decodes to                                                                                                                                       |
| --------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| starts with **`800`** | strip `800` → `components.id` (e.g. `800439` → component 439; `8002574` → component 2574). ~2,340 such                                           |
| starts with **`500`** | `buyer_products` — formula `sugarwish_id = "500" + buyer_products.id` (e.g. buyer_product id 60 → 50060; id 42 → 50042). For phantom-kit mapping |
| plain integer         | `receiver_products.product_id` OR `buyer_products.id` (do NOT assume receiver without checking)                                                  |
| `0` / `NULL`          | no mapping (**~2,338 of 7,610** templates in frozen Odoo; was ~1,371)                                                                          |

- The `sugarwish-odoo` `sale.py` filtered on `startswith('500')` / `startswith('800')`; the SugarWish integration searched Odoo products **by `product_template.sugarwish_id`** (frozen Odoo-era code).

#### `components.odoo_id` (Laravel) — FABRICATED, not a real product PK

- Formula: `components.odoo_id = "800" + components.id` (string concat, varchar; e.g. id 439 → `'800439'`, id 8 → `'8008'`). Range observed ~8008–8002763 (~2,540 rows populated). NOT a real Odoo product id.
- ❌ **AI joins `components.odoo_id` directly to `product_product.id`** → ✅ **Reality:** it matches the WRONG rows. The real round-trip is via `product_template.sugarwish_id`; the real product match is by **SKU** (`components.sku` = `default_code`).
- The "Odoo Live: Failed Products List" email showed `800xxx` values that are Laravel synthetic IDs, resolved to the real Odoo product via `sugarwish_id` (Odoo-era email).

#### `buyer_products.odoo_id` (Laravel)

- Formula: `odoo_id = "500" + buyer_products.id`. Populated on **all** ~1,216 buyer_products rows (~99% match the formula; ~12 outliers from re-mapped products). The SugarWish↔ERP product link for buyer products.

#### `odoo_id` on SERP `serp_*` tables — cross-system JOIN key

- ❌ **AI joins SERP↔Odoo on `id = id`** → ✅ **Reality:** join on the **`odoo_id` column**. The durable "SERP-origin" signal is **`odoo_id IS NULL`** = SERP-native, `IS NOT NULL` = Odoo-sourced (NOT any `id >= 1B` range). `id = odoo_id` holds ONLY for Odoo-seeded rows. Child rows created by SERP workers (stock_move, stock_move_line, quants) have `odoo_id = NULL`; since the cutover, new orders ingested by `serp_order_queue_worker` also have `odoo_id` NULL.
- Odoo is frozen, so `odoo_id` is now a historical/seed link, not a live sync key.

#### `oddo_synchronized` — note the typo

- **Legacy (renamed `oddo_synchronized(deleted)` at cutover — the old name now throws "Unknown column").** The column on Laravel `ec_order`/`preselect_orders` was misspelled **`oddo_synchronized`** (double-d, missing second-o), NOT `odoo_synchronized`. It was the Odoo-sync flag. Values in prod (pre-cutover counts): `0`=not synced (~77K), `1`=synced (~3.9M), `2`=in-flight/partial/retry (~64–166), `5`=vendor/special/bypass (~17K). **Value `3` (stuck/archived-SKU/errored) appears in WishWorks bug reports but did NOT exist in prod** (per direct query). Companion flag: `ship_date_odoo_synchronized` (also `(deleted)`; replaced by `ship_date_serp_synchronized`). Post-cutover ingest is tracked by `ec_order.serp_sync` (`0` pending, `1` synced, `4` cancelled, `5` bypass; `2` is an Odoo-era/backfill slot the worker never writes but that still counts as unsynced demand).

### Product Types & Product Lines

#### `laravel_live.product_type` — SOURCE OF TRUTH for product line

Product line is determined by **`laravel_live.product_type` (integer)**, NOT Odoo `product_category` (which scatters the same SA across multiple categories and is unreliable).

| ID      | Product Type                                        |
| ------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| 1       | Candy                                               |
| 2       | Popcorn                                             |
| 3 / 45  | Cookies / Brownies                                  |
| 5       | Snacks                                              |
| 6       | Dog Swag (Dog Treats & Swag)                        |
| 7       | Cotton Candy                                        |
| 10      | Wine Tastings                                       |
| 13      | "Customize a Sugarwish" / Custom Mug sender side    |
| 14      | Wine                                                |
| 16 / 40 | Candles / Spa                                       |
| 17 / 18 | Cocktail / Mocktail Mixers (excluded from forecast) |
| 19 / 20 | Coffee / Tea                                        |
| 25      | 12 Nights                                           |
| 39      | Gourmet Pantry                                      |
| 43      | Gift Cards (location_id NULL)                       |
| 46      | Hot Sauce                                           |
| 47      | Custom Mug & Treats (recipient side)                |
| 49      | Candy & Snacks (merged, launched 2025-07-14)        |
| 51      | Gourmet Goods & Spa (merged spa)                    |
| 550     | Vinebox                                             |
| 567     | Bakery & Cafe (launched 2025-04-14)                 |

- ❌ **AI assumes `product_category` determines product line** → ✅ **Reality:** use `laravel_live.product_type` and the SA-NN- SKU prefix number.
- **`product_type` is context-dependent**: `ec_order.product_type` classifies the _order context_ while `receiver_products.product_type` classifies the _physical product_. Same cookie ships as type 567 (Bakery & Cafe) on one order and 47 (Custom Mug & Treats) on another. For routing/fulfillment, classify by `ec_order.product_type`.
- Dashboard product-line buckets: `Cubes`=16/40/51/39, `Cups`=candy/snacks, `Cookie cartons`=cookies/brownies, `cartons`=coffee/tea.
- **Product Line (concept)** = a grouping of product_types (Laravel `product_lines` table: Treats, Drinks, The Selects, Plants, Experiences, Lifestyle, etc.).
- **Categories (2026 SKU rationalization — 5 core + niche):** Candy & Snacks, Bakery & Cafe, Gourmet Goods & Spa, Wine & Cheers, Popcorn; plus niche Hot Sauce, Dog Treats & Swag, Custom Mug & Treats.

### Gift Sizes & Size Terms

- **Pick count** = number of product selections a recipient gets ("Pick 2", "Pick 4", "Pick 6", etc.). Also the box/shipper size grouping (2-pick, 4-pick, 8-pick, 12-pick/XL, "grand", "mini").
- **`buyer_products` = "size"**: "buyer product" is a SYNONYM for "size" (refers to `size_projections_copy` rows), NOT a separate buyer-level allocation. **Buyer product** = the box/credit/SKU the sender buys.
- **`ec_order.size` is MISNAMED**: it holds a `buyer_products.id` (the box/product the recipient chose), NOT a physical size string and NOT a `size_names.id`. Correct join: `ec_order.product_type = buyer_products.product_type AND ec_order.size = buyer_products.id`. Real size lookup is `buyer_products.size_name_id`. Low buyer_product IDs collide with disabled receiver_products (e.g. 41, 61–65, 90–91, 116, 137, 353, 923) → "wrong candy on slip" bug.
- **`number_of_candies`** = integer count (2,4,6,8,12) of individual items per package — a legacy ecommerce field used as a cost-analysis/forecast scaling metric.
- **Grand orders ship as 3 boxes** via `kit_id=917` (component quantity=2 → 1 outer + 2 junior), **NOT** via `item_multiplier` (which is 1 for grands). Grand buyer_product IDs: 210, 213, 248, 262, 599, 601, 6864, 6916.
- **Tasting-kit pack size** is encoded in `product_category` id: 55=3-pack, 56=6-pack, 57=9-pack, 165=12-pack, 190=4-pack, 202/203/205/206=monthly boxes (6-pack). SERP `wine_dashboard.py` on `main` now derives tasting-kit sizes from the `(N)` pack-size suffix in the category name (so 165 and 190 are included); its DB-failure fallback hardcodes {55,56,57,165,190}.
- **Shipper** = the corrugated **outer** mailing box (sizes by pick-count: 2-/4-/8-/12-pick(XL)/grand/mini); chronic stockouts are tracked separately from product stockouts.

### Odoo-Tracked vs SERP-Tracked

Ownership distinction that predates the cutover:

- **Odoo-tracked** = standard products (the historical term; inventory formerly in Odoo `stock_quant`). Since the 2026-09-26 cutover their inventory lives in `serp_app` (`serp_stock_quant`); Odoo is frozen history.
- **SERP-tracked** = custom-branded merchandise (mugs, sleeves, apparel, t-shirts, water bottles, hats, client-provided items). Lives in the `components` table with variants/sizes, uses `component_orders` (bypassing the kit system), SERP `stock_quant`/`stock_move`, auto-PO from `buyer_orders`. **Never in Odoo** (Jason Kiefer decision, 2026-02-08: custom branding 100% through SERP, never Odoo).
- **`inventory_source`** (enum `'odoo'`/`'serp'`) marked which system authoritatively tracked a component/component order. **Renamed to `inventory_source(deleted)` at cutover (WW-2994)** in `laravel_live.components` and `component_orders` — the column is a dead marker; don't query or write it (SERP `registry.yaml` keeps the legacy shadow-component pattern `inventory_source='odoo' AND number_of_picks=0` for manage only and has a separate serp-mode twin that no longer uses the renamed column).
- Serpy formerly auto-routed product-creation ops by Odoo-tracked vs SERP-tracked ("Create Odoo-Tracked Component/Receiver Product" wrote Laravel+ERP and wired IDs; "Create SERP-Tracked Component" was SERP-only; "Create Product (Odoo Only)" skipped Laravel). Since cutover all ops route to `serp:<entity>` handlers; the op type names `create_odoo_tracked_component` / `create_odoo_tracked_receiver_product` still exist in `backend/serpy/ops/schema_types.py` (product create/update side effects were restored post-cutover), while the old SERP-tracked / Odoo-only variants are gone.
- **RM SKUs sync ONLY to the ERP (SERP now), NOT Laravel.**

### Recipe Keys

- **Recipe key** (`erecipe`/`recipe`) = an alternate lookup into a phantom BOM / kit, format `"{N}cube-bp{buyer_product_id}"` (e.g. `"1cube-bp6978"`, `"1cube-bp41"`). Stored in `merchandise_packaging_recipes.recipe_key` (UNIQUE) in WishDesk.
- Ties a buyer_product to `outer_box_sku` (e.g. `c_2`, `c_3`), `cube_size` / `merchandise_cube_size`, and add/remove component lists (`additional_components` JSON `{add:[], remove:[]}`).
- Snapshotted onto orders in `ec_order.merchandise_selections` JSON as `recipe_snapshot` (`recipe_id`, `recipe_key`, `outer_box_sku`, `buyer_product_id`, `merchandise_cube_size`, add/remove arrays). Composition is driven by recipe key → `mrp.bom` → `serp_product_product`, NOT order rows.
- ❌ **AI assumes a standalone `recipes` table exists** → ✅ **Reality:** no `recipes` table; recipe data is embedded as `recipe_snapshot` inside `branding_records.merchandise` / `ec_order.merchandise_selections` JSON. `serp_product_product` has **no `recipe_key` column** (a migration is needed for custom-branding work, WW-465).
- **`recipe_key` is distinct from the Laravel "default kit"**: the default kit (`sugarwish.kits`/`component_kits`) is the colored-box layer, while recipe-key lookup resolves the packaging/merchandise composition layer.

### Box Vocabularies (two distinct systems)

Do not conflate:

1. **RECIPE box SKUs** (`merchandise_packaging_recipes`, `merchandise_cube_size`): `c_1`, `c_1.25`, `c_1.5`, `c_2`, `c_3`, `c_4` — a packaging decision.
2. **LIVERY / SLEEVE box SKUs** (`design_boxes`, `branding_records.physical_branding.entries[].box_sku`): `a_mini`, `a_small`, `a_medium`, `a_large`, `a_xlarge`, `c_1`, `c_small`, `c_medium`, `c_large`, `c_3`, `h_small`, `h_medium` — sleeve/print sizing. Livery `SKU_TRIM_TABLE` maps box_sku → print dims; a missing entry (e.g. `c_3`, `h_*` hot-sauce keys) makes `normalizeSkuKey` THROW and silently aborts the whole sleeve PDF.

### Kit / BOM Vocabulary

- **Kit** = a bundle/assortment = **phantom BOM** (`mrp_bom.type='phantom'` / `serp_mrp_bom.type`), also called "recipe" — auto-explodes into component lines on sale/delivery (no MO). In Laravel = phantom-BOM-equivalent recipe (`kits` + `component_kits`); 89% of kits contain just 1 component (the colored box B-\*).
- **BOM (Bill of Materials)** = component recipe. **Normal BOM** = real manufacturing via a Manufacturing Order (MO), reduced only via manual MO, does NOT auto-explode. `serp_mrp_bom.type` = ENUM(`normal`,`phantom`) (no third value).
- **Multiple kit sources can disagree:** Laravel `kits`/`component_kits`, SERP phantom BOM, frozen Odoo `mrp_bom`. The box that ships often comes from the SERP phantom BOM while the Laravel kit is empty. SERP UI: `serp.sugarwish.com/kits`. The `laravel_live.serp_mrp_bom` copy + SerpBomService on-order kit explosion is the intended current SoT for kits; normal BOMs live in `serp_app.serp_mrp_bom`.
- Carolyn Pardee: "Kits and BOMs are the same thing when it comes to Odoo" — but architecturally **additive/complementary**: SERP/Odoo phantom BOMs cover everything EXCEPT the colored box (S-/P-/I-/E-/L-/T-/C-), Laravel kits cover ONLY the colored box (B-).
- **MO (Manufacturing Order)** = `mrp.production` / SERP's `serp_mrp_production`; instruction to produce a qty via a normal BOM (RM→SA). Phantom BOMs create zero MOs.
- **Prelist** = a "soft"/intent MO — a product SugarWish has the raw material (RM) for and wants to make sellable (SA) live on the website **before the real MO is actually cut**. Records the build intent (product + qty against the exact BOM) so the SA can go live now; a real MO is cut later, and the PO-create flow offers to unbuild matching prelists alongside MOs. Tracked as `receiver_products.prelist_qty` in `laravel_live` (WW-1667, SERPY op + SERP feature) with `serp_app.prelist_qty_log` as the change log; forecast inventory nets `prelist_qty` (display-only field on `crud_forecast_inventory`).
- **Component** = an individual item in a recipe (raw material or packaging). **Receiver product** = an individual flavor/item the recipient picks.
- **Popcorn phantom explode — RETIRED 2026-09-01 (PR #1056):** the `normal→phantom` SA-02 seed flip was DELETED; popcorn BOMs stay `normal`. Popcorn forecast still splits by vendor **CityPop vs Poppin** (no collapse).
- **City Pop / Poppin & Mixin** = popcorn vendor sub-brands; normalize to "Popcorn" in PTK but split as `Popcorn@CityPop` vs `Popcorn@Poppin` in forecast (Jack explicitly does NOT collapse Popcorn).
- **Bakery & Cafe split** = product_type 567 split into `Bakery&Cafe@EW` / `Bakery&Cafe@TY` in the SERP forecast (driven by `product_type` + `location_id` of the **first item** in the order).

### Order / Fulfillment Terms

| Table              | Purpose                                                                         |
| ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `giftcards_card`   | eCard before redemption; source of truth for sender/receiver email              |
| `ec_order`         | Redeemed receiver order (one shipment); `size` = `buyer_products.id` (MISNAMED) |
| `items`            | Recipient's chosen flavors (one row per flavor)                                 |
| `preselect_orders` | Buyer pre-selects contents + recipient address                                  |
| `component_orders` | Box/packaging component lines (`inventory_source` marker deleted at cutover)    |

- **Receiver order** = recipient redeems an eCard and chooses treats (order numbers start with **200**; `sw_id < 600,000,000`; `ec_order`).
- **eCard** = the digital gift sent to a recipient (`giftcards_card` row, pre-redemption). **Redemption** = recipient picks the gift (creates `ec_order`); products are resolved at **redemption time**, not creation — so historical/consolidated SKUs must be backfilled. Since the cutover, `serp_order_queue_worker` ingests `ec_order` → `serp_sale_order` (`odoo_id` NULL) (the worker routes on `serp_sale_order.ec_order_id` vs `preselect_order_id`, not on `sw_id`).
- **Preselect / Pre-pick / prepick / wholesale:** buyer pre-selects contents AND recipient address up front (order numbers start with **6000**; `sw_id ≥ 600,000,000`; `preselect_orders`, bridged via `preselect_order_id`, NOT `ec_order`). Often bulk B2B. Bypasses cart and drop-level; promo codes do NOT apply; ship-dated prepicks prioritized over FIFO. Disabled site-wide Oct 2021 (caused majority of stockouts despite <4% of sales); later relaunched. Standard eCard = recipient chooses.
- **Sweet-shoppe / Sweetificate order** = self-purchase / recipient-selectable types (`items.order_type='sweet-shoppe-order'`; `preselect_orders.type` enum `preselect`/`sweet-shoppe`/`sweetificate`).
- **Merchandise order** = a distinct `order_type` SERP handles natively (SERP-tracked), JSON-driven (branding_record = offered, `ec_order.merchandise_selections` = chosen).
- **WishLink:** shareable redemption link (`giftcards_card.delivery_method='wishlink'`; prefer `delivery_method`; the boolean column is `wishlink`, there is no `is_wishlink`). Priced $2.50/card OR 10%-down + charge-on-redemption (older sources say $2/link — verify current price). Single-Use or Multi-Use with optional domain restrictions. Was non-refundable; changed 2024 to "cancel and credit −10%" like eCards.
- **Prepay programs:** **HHS** = Holiday Head Start; **PPS** = Pre-Pay/budgeted prepay. Client pre-pays a lump sum → tiered bonus credit (~18% effective vs 15% standard large-order discount). Triggers/renews **"SugarWish Premium"** status.
- **Cancel and credit** = the default "undo" — 90% account credit (minus 10%), NOT a card refund. **Refund** = the rarer money-back-to-card path. **Redeem only** = pay 10% upfront, 90% on redemption only (not eligible for 90% credit if cancelled before redemption).
- **Placeholder order** = a pre-picked order parked because its SKU is OOS, auto-filed to `#placeholder-orders` for manual warehouse placement. **Replacement order** = reshipment of a lost/damaged/wrong item via `sugarwish.com/products/replacement`.
- **Custom Shoppe / Custom Shop** = custom-branded-product storefront (logo'd mugs, merch). Own Retool Location. Team: Sophie (+ Neal until he left 2026-09-10). Generates custom-shop PDF + sleeve PDF.
- **sw_fulfill** = SugarWish-in-house vs vendor fulfillment flag (1 = in-house assembles & ships, 0 = vendor/dropship, NULL = legacy/unknown). NOT a shipping/label status.
- **is_printed** = production-slip/label state (0/1 normal printed states; 2 = "Issue" from PDF-cron failure / missing boxcard or hi-res image; 3 = address/label-blocked or On Hold queue; 5 = added to print group). Multi-state integer, not a boolean.
- **Production slip** = the warehouse pick/pack sheet (PDF), batch-printed/merged; custom items get a 2-page slip (product slip + sleeve slip).
- **Sleeve** = printed cardboard wrap around a gift box (`branding_records.physical_branding`); matched by `buyer_product_id` (= `ec_order.size`) against `entries[].buyer_product_ids[]`. Jason calls custom sleeves "the biggest near-term revenue opportunity." Launched as a consumable with NO inventory tracking (Feb 2026); ~$2–5 each, MOQ ~1000, ~7–9 days after art approval.
- **Glitch** = an operational incident (non-dev staff file it; devs replicate, then a separate **Bug** ticket must be Product-prioritized). **Feature** = a new-capability request.

### Warehouse Locations

- **EW** = Englewood, CO warehouse (`location_id 1`, default, suffix `-E`; HQ 8450 Highfield Pkwy Ste 100, Englewood CO 80112). **TY** = Taylor, MI warehouse (`location_id 2`, suffix `-A`; 21740 Trolley Industrial Dr Ste 5, Taylor MI 48180) — the Taylor ship-from gate was retired at cutover (every `vinebox_states.ship_from` = 1). Stored in legacy `locations` table — **no FK** to `serp_stock_location`.
- Stock-location ids in `serp_stock_location`: EW/Stock/Fulfillment = 2008, TY/Stock/Fulfillment = 2006 (Odoo-era ids carried over, `odoo_id` = `id`); id 5 is `Partner Locations/Customers`, not a warehouse.
- 8 Laravel fulfillment "locations": 1=Englewood, 2=Taylor, 3=City Pop, 4=Simple Times, 5=WCC, 6=Vista, 7=Becket and Quill, 8=Poppin & Mixin.
- Picking name prefixes: `EW/OUT`/`EW/IN`/`EW/MO`/`EW/INT`; worker-created outbound named `EW/OUT/%` origin `RO-%`/`PSO-%`.

### Forecast / Inventory Terms

- **Forecast / Supplier Forecast** = the 25-week (some sources ~60-week) RM-demand planner (SERP Forecast app, teal sidebar, `serp.sugarwish.com/forecast/live-products`). Supplier forecast now pulls from SERP data (9/28).
- **Forecasting RM vs SA vs Carton:** SA runout depends only on its own RMs (not cartons); `total_inventory` is per-RM-row.
- **PTK / product_type_key** = a `"ProductType|Size"` grouping string; dashboard and `sa_projections.sql` must produce IDENTICAL strings or demand won't roll up. Popcorn sub-brands "City Pop"/"Poppin & Mixin" normalize to "Popcorn". `rm_weekly_demand_cache` uses plural `product_type_keys`.
- **Forecast color code (`/forecast/live-products`):** **GREEN** = inventory available; **ORANGE** = SA (manufactured) runs out but RM remains; **YELLOW** = on-hand runs out but incoming PO not arrived; **RED** = all inventory runs out.
- **Drop level** = inventory floor at/below which a SKU is auto-disabled (`components.drop_level` was deleted at cutover along with other component inventory columns; `receiver_products.drop_level` remains). **Threshold** = alert trigger, typically ≈2× drop_level.
- **Core SKU** = must stay live (`receiver_products.sku_type='core'`, `is_core=1`, ~220–275 SA SKUs). **Seasonal** (`sku_type='seasonal'`) and **Legacy** (`sku_type='legacy'`, default) run out and are not reordered; legacy is excluded from the core %. Business rule: `is_core` must equal `(sku_type=='core')`, enforced in app code only (helper `core_flag_for_sku_type`), no DB trigger; no live mismatches currently (product_id 4177, SA-46-033 was the earlier exception). Live count: 398 `is_core=1` receiver_products (298 enabled).
- **Equivalency ratio** = cost-normalization multiplier per product type so executives can compare across lines (Candy & Snacks = 1.0 baseline, Popcorn = 0.5, Gourmet Pantry / Spa ≈ 0.75, Wine/Gift Sets TBD).
- **Inventory Days** = current inventory / (L7D Use / 7) (Jason originated). **packing_goal** = min SA-days for core SKUs with RM available; **purchase_goal** = min total-days for core SKUs by product line.
- **SA / RM inventory — where it lives:** **RM** (and on-hand / valuation) = `serp_app` (`serp_stock_quant`, SVL). **SA sellable** = Laravel; readers are moving to the laravel_live VIEW `receiver_product_inventory.sellable_qty`, fed from `serp_published_stock` (BASE TABLE in laravel_live, as is `serp_published_kits`), rebuilt by `serp_order_queue_worker`. `receiver_products.inventory_qty` (and `mi_inventory_qty`, `odoo_inventory`) were renamed `…(deleted)` at cutover — the columns are dead; don't read or write them. The Odoo→Laravel `SynchronizeOdooInventory` sync is dead with Odoo frozen.

### System & Product Names

| Name               | What it is                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **SERP**           | SugarWish's in-house ERP (Jack Kiefer's build, Next.js + FastAPI/Python), a from-scratch clone of Odoo 15's ORM held to line-by-line parity. **Replaced Odoo at the 2026-09-26 cutover.** Production data lives in **`serp_app`** (MySQL, Hetzner `5.161.233.240`); `serp_test` is staging. Runs on the Hetzner K3s cluster; every push to `main` auto-deploys (Jenkins → `deploy-k8s.sh` → ArgoCD). NOT the same as "Serpy".                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **serp_app / serp_test** | `serp_app` = LIVE/production SERP DB and ERP source of truth. `serp_test` = STAGING SERP (serp-dev); a real DB, not a throwaway and NOT production. Migration heads are independent (a gap is normal).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| **Serpy**          | SERP's **AI ops agent** (Slack bot `U096P936NQ7` "SERPY Dev" + web UI `serp.sugarwish.com/serpy/<draft_id>`). Plain-language ops → JSON operations → SERP/Laravel behind a Slack approval flow (previously also Odoo via XML-RPC; the Odoo XML-RPC handler arms were deleted in SERP commit `90e712f39` on 2026-09-26; only the read-only `odoo_xmlrpc_service.py` remains). Generates numbered "Drafts" (e.g. "Draft #683", stored in `serpy_drafts`); ops queue in `serpy_sync_queue` (`serp_app`), processed by `workers/serpy_sync_worker.py`.
