"x/y synced" = x ops applied to SERP, y = total ops (partial = SERP-side validation rejected some; before 9/26 it meant Odoo-side rejection). **NOT a person and NOT a typo for SERP.** "Replace SKU X with Y" should mean a kit **component swap** (remove old + add new across all kits), not archive-old/activate-new.                                                                                                                                                                                |
| **serpy_sync_queue** | Serpy's op queue in `serp_app` (formerly `odoo_sync_queue_live`; drafts table formerly `serp_draft_operations_live`, now `serpy_drafts`). Legacy name **`odoo_sync_queue`** survives only as frozen Retool copies (`odoo_sync_queue*`) — Retool is NOT the sync engine anymore. Live `sync_target` values since 9/26: `serp`, `multi`, `laravel` (last `odoo` target created 2026-09-25).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **darklaunch**     | **RETIRED at the 2026-09-26 cutover.** Was SERP's dual-write shadow-validation system (SERP and Odoo side-by-side, gated at "<1% drift, stable 2 weeks", validated by `/compare-darklaunch`; UNRELATED to feature-flag/canary darklaunches). The drift-monitor package is deleted; the `darklaunch-drift`, `publish-serp-kits`, `reconcile-published-stock` crons are inactive; `serp_darklaunch_meta` was dropped 2026-08-03; the `SERP_DARKLAUNCH_ENABLED`, `SERP_SHADOW_WRITES_ENABLED`, `USE_SERP_AS_LIVE` and `ENVIRONMENT_SET` flags are gone from `backend/`. n8n "Darklaunch Compare" is inactive. Local `*_darklaunch`/`*_replica` DB names are legacy (local DBs are now `serp_local_staging` / `serp_local_prod`). Kept as history and as Odoo-parity notes. |
| **manage**         | The Laravel admin/management app **and** its staging MySQL database (`manage.sugarwish.com`). Renamed 2026-05-11 from "laravel staging". Near-identical schema to `laravel_live` (~8% of prod volume). Hosts SERP's upstream ORM tables; Serpy writes `receiver_products` here in staging (NOT prod `laravel_live`). "Test on manage" = the Laravel staging environment. Its embedded `serp_*` tables are stale (NOT live SERP data).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **livery / SWOP**  | "Sugarwish Operations Platform" — Cris (Criston) Sloan's repo (`csloan-sw/livery`). Dual-natured: (1) the warehouse **custom-shop print station / sleeve imposition** backbone (generates sleeve & custom-shop-slip PDFs), and (2) an **MCP ops-tooling suite** (`mcp-db-tool`, `mcp-slack`, `mcp-wishdesk`, `swim-kb`, `custom-shop-slip`). **Reads the mug image from `ec_order.merchandise_selections.items[N].design_selected.print_image_url`** (matched by `item_id`), NOT `branding_records.merchandise`. Caches PDFs by `orderId` ONLY and never invalidates on branding edits — after a data fix the operator MUST click "Regenerate" (POST `/reset-status/:orderId`) then re-run generate-batch. Separate from sw-cortex.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| **WishDesk**       | SugarWish's CS/CRM/design/billing platform. The product brand for the **SWAC** repo.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| **SWAC**           | GitHub repo `jasonbkiefer/SWAC` ("SugarWish Activity Coordinator" — the literal name is misleading) = **the WishDesk app** (React + Express + TypeScript + Drizzle + MySQL). NOT a separate system from WishDesk. Envs: `desk.sugarwish.com` (live, branch `live`), `desk2` (dev, branch `development`), `desk3` (staging — but points at LIVE databases, not isolated). Branch flow: feature → development → staging → live (NOT main). Times stored in **Mountain Time, not UTC**.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| **SWIM**           | WishDesk-embedded AI customer-service chatbot, powered by the `kb-v2` Qdrant collection. Generates draft replies (acceptance rate ~1%). **Model: Anthropic `claude-sonnet-4-6`** (default via `ANTHROPIC_CHAT_MODEL`, unset in `.env` so the code default in `server/swim/shared/config.ts:156` applies) — same model for agent-response-suggestion drafts (`server/services/agent-response-service.ts:93`) and SWIM email-processing draft generation (`server/swim/workflows/email-processing/draft-generation.ts:188`). The **Gmail/Gift-Concierge email drafter is a separate stack on OpenAI** (`GMAIL_DRAFT_AI_MODEL`, falling back to `OPENAI_GENERATION_MODEL` with no code default; `gpt-5.1` was the value last seen in `.env`; `server/services/gmail/gmail-ai-draft-generator.ts`) — don't conflate it with SWIM/Ask Wishy. `.env`'s `ANTHROPIC_GENERATION_MODEL=claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` only feeds the release-notes AI drafter — a red herring when hunting the chatbot's model var (model facts as last checked in code). |
| **SWIRL**          | Two roles: (1) **Sugarwish Intelligence Reference Library** — Jason's org-wide AI knowledge platform (docs + MCP access + Slack bot); (2) the **WishWorks datastore** (auto-generated ticket commits, Jason's private `jasonbkiefer/SWIRL` repo). SEPARATE from Jack's `sw-cortex`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| **WishWorks (WW)** | In-house ticket/glitch tracker (`WW-####` tickets), managed via the **WishBot** AI Slack bot; replaced glitch reports March 2026. The ticket "track" field determines repo/team ownership.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| **Odoo**           | The PostgreSQL ERP (v15, cloud-hosted on Odoo.sh, no shell access). **FROZEN read-only history since the 2026-09-26 cutover** (last write on `purchase_order` 2026-09-25 21:12); stale for anything after 9/26. No longer drives orders, inventory, or accounting — SERP (`serp_app`) does. Being disconnected from SERP/Laravel code (removal in progress).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| **Laravel**        | The main e-commerce app & order domain (`sugarwish-laravel`); `laravel_live` is the production orders DB. NOT "the SERP database" — it co-hosts a thin `serp_*` bridge (`serp_published_kits`, `serp_published_stock`, `serp_mrp_bom`); other `serp_*` tables there are planned for removal.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| **Retool**         | Low-code analytics/ops platform (PostgreSQL); hosts AI observability, auth bridge, forecast caches, frozen legacy copies of the Serpy sync queues (`odoo_sync_queue*`), and a stale set of QuickBooks/Stripe/Shopify reporting mirrors. No longer the sync engine.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| **n8n**            | Self-hosted automation platform (`n8n.sugarwish.com`); runs Slack alerts and monitors. Odoo-era workflows (Odoo Sync Error Handler, Odoo Order Sync Integrity Monitor, Sync Receiver Product Inventory with Odoo, Disable Unreserved Products V2, Odoo inventory in wrong location) were **disabled at cutover** and replaced by `… - Cutover` variants (Disable Unreserved Products - Cutover, Core SKU Live Warnings - Cutover, Inventory In Wrong Location - Cutover, active) plus `Sheets Export - serp_app`. There is no code-generated "Darklaunch Drift Monitor" workflow in the live list (the drift monitor is retired).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |

### Reserve, Seasonal, Sync & Tooling Terms

- **Holiday Reserve / reserve orders** = enterprise pre-sold holiday gifts (2026: ~14,000 gifts, ~$1.3M across 66 reservations, launched July). They are fulfilled in Nov/Dec from the specific branded component SKU tied to the kit (not raw materials). Reserve recipes require merchandise type, product size, number of flexible extras and number of soft extras (soft/flexible extras fit the signature-item bay or spare cube space; fixed cube extras take a full cube and set the box size). Reserve demand is added to the supplier forecast as an editable "holiday reserve" sheet (weeks 49-50; data from WishDesk, cohorts in `forecast_reserve_cohorts`) because peak projections exclude reserve orders. The SERPY **Kits Monitor** posts recipe-coverage findings (`NO_RECIPE` = a recipe key with no recipe) to the `#reserve-order` channel.
- **Peak planning** = holiday peak (Dec ~3-20) sizing: supplier buffer locked by early Oct, FTE calculator, and load tests of the SERP order queue (production ran on a single dedicated server with the order queue as its own process as of 9/29).
- **Seasonal packaging** = a dated alternate box: `buyer_products.seasonal_kit` is used instead of `default_kit` when the order's `seasonal_kit_until` (on `giftcards_card` / `cart`, NOT on `buyer_products`) is set and the order falls on or before that date. Production slips show seasonal packaging visibly; SERP kits are being extended with a general seasonal-replacement mechanism.
- **`ec_order.serp_sync`** = post-cutover ingest flag: `0` pending, `1` synced, `4` cancelled, `5` bypass (drop-ship/vendor), `2` unsynced backfill slot (rare; still counts as unsynced demand). Set by `serp_order_queue_worker`.
- **Published stock / published kits** = `serp_published_stock` (~4.9K rows) and `serp_published_kits` (~635 rows) in `laravel_live`: aggregates rebuilt by `serp_order_queue_worker` from `serp_app` and read by the `receiver_product_inventory` view; the only `serp_*` tables planned to remain in `laravel_live`.
- **WCC Ledger** (`serp.sugarwish.com/forecast/wcc-ledger`) = the WCC/VineBox partner-inventory reconciliation page over `serp_wcc_ledger_*` tables (baselines from SERP on-hand counts, Finale snapshots, VineBox ShipStation shipments, per-SKU issue status); VineBox kit rows show whether kits are built and in stock.
- **serp-staging** = browse-only SERP copy (branch `staging`) that reads prod `serp_app` with no workers/crons/migrations; distinct from `serp_test` (the serp-dev DB).
- **`- Cutover` n8n workflows** = active replacements for the Odoo-era workflows disabled at cutover (Disable Unreserved Products, Core SKU Live Warnings, Inventory In Wrong Location).
- **PR poller / 🚀 (rocket)** = SERP dev tooling (`scripts/pr-review-poller.sh`): reviews announced PRs into `dev`, 🚀-reacted PRs first; Jack's `:rocket:` reaction in the PR's Slack thread is itself an approval and `rocket-merge.sh` merges that commit whatever the reviews said (GitHub still refuses conflicts).

### Prefix / Domain Notes

- **manage** vs `laravel_live` vs `serp_app`: always resolve WHICH DB before querying a `serp_*` table — the same table name can exist in `laravel_live` (live order path, `/kits`) and `serp_app` (ERP) with different contents.
- **SERP ↔ Odoo naming that remains** (do NOT "fix"): the legacy `oddo_synchronized` spelling (column now `(deleted)`, history only); `odoo_id` join key; `odoo_id IS NULL` = SERP-native; `ENVIRONMENT_SET` survives only in `deploy-k8s-staging.sh` (config derives from `DETECTED_ENVIRONMENT`).

## Claude Code MCP Tool Reference (GitHub, Slack, Logs, n8n, Session Mesh, Knowledge)

Tool catalogs for the MCP servers wired into every Claude Code session via `~/.mcp.json`.
These moved out of the always-on `~/CLAUDE.md` so they cost nothing until a session
actually needs them — search this section when you need the exact tool name or arguments.
(Database access — `mcp__db__*` — deliberately stays inline in `~/CLAUDE.md`, because it
carries the mandatory describe-first HARD GATE that prevents column-guessing.)
`~/.mcp.json` wires eight servers: `db`, `github`, `slack-search`, `jack-slack`, `logs`, `knowledge`, `n8n`, `sessions`. `db` exposes `list_databases`, `list_tables`, `describe_table`, `query_database` and `query_database_from_file`; the other seven are catalogued below. The hub session also has claude.ai connectors (`mcp__claude_ai_Slack__*`, Gmail, Google Calendar/Drive, Claude Docs) and `claude-in-chrome`, which are NOT in `~/.mcp.json`.

### GitHub (`mcp__github__*`) — read-only repo access

| Need to...     | Do this                                              |
| -------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| List repos     | `mcp__github__list_repos`                            |
| Search code    | `mcp__github__search_code { query, repo?, limit? }`  |
| Get file       | `mcp__github__get_file { repo, path, ref? }`         |
| List files     | `mcp__github__list_files { repo, path?, ref? }`      |
| List branches  | `mcp__github__list_branches { repo, limit? }`        |
| List commits   | `mcp__github__list_commits { repo, branch?, path?, limit? }` |
| List PRs       | `mcp__github__list_pull_requests { repo, state?, limit? }` |
| Get PR details | `mcp__github__get_pull_request { repo, pr_number }`  |
| Repo info      | `mcp__github__get_repo_info { repo }`                |

**Always pass `ref`** — without it these default to the repo's default branch, which is
usually NOT the branch you want. SERP: prod `main`, dev `dev`. SWAC: prod `live`, dev
`development`, staging `staging`. sugarwish-odoo: prod `main`, staging `staging_new`.
sugarwish-laravel: prod `blue`. Never assume `main` is correct.
**HARD RULE:** never use `search_code` on a locally-checked-out repo (SERP/SWAC/sw-cortex)
— use `rg`/Explore. It uses GitHub query grammar, not substrings.

### Slack search (`mcp__slack-search__*`) — semantic search over history

| Need to...        | Do this                                                                  |
| ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Search messages   | `mcp__slack-search__search_slack_messages { query, limit?, channelId?, minScore?, afterDate?, beforeDate? }` |
| Get context       | `mcp__slack-search__get_slack_context { channelId, timestamp, windowMinutes?, limit? }` |
| Get thread        | `mcp__slack-search__get_slack_thread { channelId, threadTs, limit? }`    |
| Check sync status | `mcp__slack-search__get_slack_sync_status`                               |

Workflow: search first, then pull context on interesting hits using the returned
`channelId` + `timestamp`. Indexing syncs via `npm run slack:sync` in sw-cortex.

### Slack posting / reading (`mcp__jack-slack__*`)

| Need to...           | Do this                                                         |
| -------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Post a message       | `mcp__jack-slack__slack_post_message { channel, text }`         |
| Reply in a thread    | `mcp__jack-slack__slack_reply_to_thread { channel, thread_ts }` |
| Read channel history | `mcp__jack-slack__slack_get_channel_history { channel }`        |
| Read thread replies  | `mcp__jack-slack__slack_get_thread_replies { channel, ts }`     |
| List channels        | `mcp__jack-slack__slack_list_channels`                          |
| Look up user / users | `mcp__jack-slack__slack_get_user_profile` / `slack_get_users`   |
| Add a reaction       | `mcp__jack-slack__slack_add_reaction { channel, ts, name }`     |

**Identity rule (still enforced in `~/CLAUDE.md`):** automated/agent posts ALWAYS go
through `mcp__jack-slack__*` (posts as the jackbot app), NEVER
`mcp__claude_ai_Slack__slack_send_message`, which posts as Jack the human.

### Logs (`mcp__logs__*`) — sw-cortex service logs

| Need to...     | Do this                                                        |
| -------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Search logs    | `mcp__logs__search_logs { service?, level?, search?, since?, limit? }` |
| Recent logs    | `mcp__logs__get_recent_logs { limit? }`                        |
| Recent errors  | `mcp__logs__get_recent_errors { limit? }`                      |
| Log statistics | `mcp__logs__get_log_stats`                                     |

### n8n (`mcp__n8n__*`) — live self-hosted instance, read-only

| Need to...            | Do this                                                      |
| --------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------ |
| List workflows        | `mcp__n8n__list_workflows { active?, limit? }`               |
| Get one workflow JSON | `mcp__n8n__get_workflow { id }`                              |
| List recent runs      | `mcp__n8n__list_executions { workflowId?, status?, limit? }` |
| Get one run's detail  | `mcp__n8n__get_execution { id, include_data? }`              |

Reads the ACTUAL running workflows via the REST API — always current, unlike the stale
JSON exports under `workflows/n8n/`. `status` is `success` | `error` | `waiting`.

### Session Mesh (`mcp__sessions__*`) — see/coordinate with other running sessions

| Need to...                              | Do this                                           |
| --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- |
| See every running session + its task    | `mcp__sessions__list_sessions`                    |
| Check if a peer is already on your task | `mcp__sessions__check_overlap { task }`           |
| Read a peer's recent output             | `mcp__sessions__read_session { paneId, lines? }`  |
| Message a peer session                  | `mcp__sessions__message_session { target, text }` |

Each entry carries the peer's friendly name and `paneId` (e.g. `w8:p2C`) — either works as the `target`/`paneId` for
read/message (prefer the name). `status` is `working` | `idle` | `done` | `blocked`; `task` is the peer's live
tab title. The coordination POLICY (check overlap on launch, one heads-up, never chatter,
never drive another session) stays in `~/CLAUDE.md` — it is behavior, not lookup.
Backed by the `herdr agent` socket API; needs the Herdr server up.

### Knowledge base (`mcp__knowledge__*`)

| Need to...               | Do this                                              |
| ------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| Search the KB            | `mcp__knowledge__search_knowledge { query, limit? }` |
| Expand truncated section | `mcp__knowledge__get_knowledge_section { section }`  |

Indexes `DICTIONARY.md` + `knowledge/COLUMN_MANIFEST.md` (overridable via the
`KNOWLEDGE_FILES` env var, which REPLACES the defaults rather than extending them).

## Claude Code token spend — measured cost model (2026-08-25)

Where Claude Code tokens actually go, measured across all 780 usage-bearing transcripts in
`~/.claude/projects` (27.2B cache-read tokens). Recorded here so the analysis is not
re-derived from scratch every time the question comes up.

### The shape of the spend

| component    | share |
| ------------ | ----- |
| cache READS  | 74%   |
| cache writes | 18%   |
| output       | 8%    |

**Cost is dominated by context RE-READING, not by work done.** Every turn re-sends the whole
accumulated conversation, so cache-read per turn climbs with session length:

| session length | cache-read per turn |
| -------------- | ------------------- |
| < 50 turns     | 92k                 |
| 50-150         | 157k                |
| 150-400        | 234k                |
| 400-1000       | 362k                |
| 1000+          | **457k**            |

Cost per turn quintuples, so a session's TOTAL cost grows roughly with the **square of its
turn count**. Concentration follows: the 63 sessions over 400 turns (8% of all sessions)
burned **56% of all cache-read tokens ever spent**; sessions under 50 turns were 1.3%.
Simulated per-turn caps: a 200k cap saves 36.3%, 150k saves 49.0%, 300k saves 18.9%.

**Practical consequence:** session LENGTH is the dominant lever, model/effort tier is second,
and instruction-file size is a distant third (the always-on baseline was 11.9% of SERP
cache-read). A fresh session per task beats a long-running one by a wide margin.

### Answered: `attachment` records are NOT billed input

In the largest transcript, `attachment` records were 2,166 entries / 6.5 MB / 17% of the file
on disk — which looks alarming until you check what they are: 1,285 `hook_success` (hook
stdout, mostly empty `content`), 800 `total_tokens_reminder`, and a long tail of
`skill_listing` / `deferred_tools_delta` / `agent_listing_delta`. They are a **distinct
record type** from `user`/`assistant`, and only 4 of 2,166 contained a usage block. They are
local harness/UI bookkeeping, **not replayed as API input**.

**So do NOT trim hook output to save tokens** — it saves nothing and would break the
tab-status and write-guard signals that depend on hook stdout. On-disk transcript size is a
poor proxy for billed context generally: base64 screenshots ARE billed and re-sent every
turn (39% of that same transcript), while these attachment records are not.

### Decided: KEEP `opus[1m]` — do not drop to a 200k window

`~/.claude/settings.json` was set to `"model": "opus[1m]"` when this was measured (it now reads `"model": "opus"`, with `effortLevel` `xhigh` and a per-model `claude-opus-5` override of `high`). The 1M window is what _permits_ a
session to reach 967k instead of hitting a wall that forces a hand-off, so it looks like the
obvious thing to cut. **It was measured and rejected.**

Peak-context distribution across 782 sessions: p50 = 161k, p90 = 418k, p99 = 792k, max 988k.

| peaked over | sessions      |
| ----------- | ------------- |
| 200k        | 322 (**41%**) |
| 300k        | 169 (22%)     |
| 400k        | 88 (11%)      |
| 500k        | 55 (7%)       |
| 700k        | 13 (1.7%)     |

**41% of sessions exceed 200k.** A hard 200k window would fail four in ten sessions
mid-task — a workflow regression, not a token optimization, and the failure lands
unpredictably in the middle of real work (large diff reviews, wide refactors). The right
control is the _behavioral_ budget (compact ~150k / hand off ~250k), which reduces the same
tokens without a hard failure mode: it makes long sessions rare instead of impossible.

Revisit only after the context-budget policy has real data behind it — if the peak
distribution shifts down and the >200k tail thins out, the 1M tier becomes harmless headroom
rather than an enabler, and the question stops mattering either way.

### What actually reduces spend

1. **Shorter sessions** — compact ~150k, hand off ~250k, one task per session.
2. **Cheap models for fan-out** — haiku/sonnet researchers, Opus for synthesis and for
   writing production code. Research agents are cheap-tier; code authors and the pre-PR
   review gate are not.
3. **Fewer/narrower reads** — grep before Read, `offset`/`limit` on big files, screenshots
   only to verify.
4. **Path-scoped rules** — a rules file without `paths:` frontmatter loads into every
   session in the repo.
